title: Competitor Comparison
description: How Myrm compares to popular AI agent platforms and what you gain by migrating.
---

# Competitor Comparison & Migration Guide

Myrm is designed as a complete AI agent workspace. Unlike terminal-only coding agents, Myrm provides a GUI-first experience with persistent sandboxes, cross-session memory, and enterprise-grade security — all while maintaining full coding capabilities.

<Note>
**Migration confidence** — You keep control: pick SaaS, self-hosted, or desktop; import Hermes skills via GUI ZIP; compare channels, memory, and security row-by-row below before you commit. For plugin-centric users (for example Grok Build), Myrm installs a **full Agent package** in one step (model + skills + MCP + subagents + safety profile), instead of making you assemble multiple plugins manually. Docs are available in **6 languages** (zh/en/ja/ko/de/zh-TW); the app auto-detects your browser language on first visit (RFC 7231 Accept-Language negotiation) across all deployment modes — WebUI, Cloud-hosted, and Tauri desktop.
</Note>

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-22 · "A Few Bots in One Group Chat, Talking to Each Other and Improving Themselves" · Grok Bot's Self-Evolution Story, and How You Decide a Round Actually Got Better · The Comments Ask the Four Hardest Questions About Agent Self-Improvement — We Have a Counterpart for Each, Plus Two Honest Gaps of Our Own)

- **What triggered this**: X @dingyi (`https://x.com/dingyi/status/2108395750128542180`, 2026-10-09 11:14; **27K views / 23 replies / 6 reposts / 143 likes / 60 bookmarks**): more and more people are discovering that Grok Bot can "really evolve itself" — pull a few bots into one group chat, let them talk to each other, they optimize themselves, and "I don't even need to intervene". The same thread claims Cursor's web cloud computer and its bot are "the same thing, just a different interaction surface", and frames the industry as three trends (VS Code shell, Codex, Bot), two of which it credits to Cursor. We extracted the full post and **23 replies** with ChromeAgent :9410 + chrome-devtools MCP.
- **Honest boundary**: **Grok Bot and Cursor are closed-source and we have no source mirror**, so we make **no assertion about how they work** — we only record what the post claims. The post carries **no checkable metric at all**: no token count, no latency, no before/after, no eval set, no success rate.
- **What the comments asked — more useful than the post itself**: the four questions that matter for any self-evolving agent:
  1. **Criterion** — @Thomas3cnl (**445 likes**): "then which step of the bots' conversation counts as 'this round got better'? the group chat keeps growing, and with nothing that can judge right or wrong, they cannot tell whether they got better or just got more into the conversation."
  2. **Deliverable** — @0xYaCo (**497 likes**): "apart from optimizing itself, what can a few chatting bots actually deliver?"
  3. **Independence** — @0xArisUniverse: "group-chat peer review ends up as inbreeding — without external input, where does the evolution come from?"
  4. **Consensus collapse** — @An_yhl (98 likes): "will a few bots end up just flattering each other?"
  Also worth recording: @CrazySoyo (328 likes) on the bot pulling a team of different AIs out of Cursor to argue — first agreeing, then tearing each other apart; @ren3634421 (701 likes) on self-evolution meaning you pay the electricity bill yourself; @PANewsLab (655 likes) "it's good, but the plans are a mess"; @DesignerBingyi (571 likes) on the gap between product thinking and engineering thinking.
- **Our side (checked against real code)** — each of the four questions has a counterpart on our side:
  - **"Did this round get better" → an improvement gate that puts the original up as baseline**: `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/skills/evolution/core/engine.py:241-253` injects the original skill as a baseline candidate into the evaluation and **skips the evolution when no variant scores higher**; the FIX path is symmetric (`engine.py:194`, `engine.py:271`).
  - **"Better, or just more talk" → a regression-case gate**: `agent/skills/evolution/core/eval_regression.py:34` guards only the cases the original already passes; every guarded case a variant loses costs 0.15 (`eval_regression.py:30`), and losing all of them is a hard filter.
  - **"Flattery / fake pass" → hollow-test detection**: `agent/skills/evolution/execution/hollow_detector.py:40` together with `execution/sandbox_validator.py:140-146` rejects trivial assertions, dummy prints and vacuous verification scripts.
  - **"What does it deliver besides self-optimizing" → output must pass physical evidence**: `agent/goals/verification/gatekeeper.py:44` verifies against criteria and `agent/goals/verification/fault_attribution.py:132` separates application / compile / test-assertion faults — "done" is not something the model gets to declare.
  - **The pipeline is wired in production**: `agent/skills/evolution/infra/integration.py:214` builds the engine; the server exposes `app/api/skills/evolution/derive.py:55` and `app/api/skills/evolution/fix.py:45`; a human review gate sits in front (`app/services/agent/confidence_approval_flow.py:52`, auto-merge threshold 0.8).
- **Two honest gaps of our own (not delivered, not advertised)**:
  1. **The reviewer and the proposer are the same model** — `agent/skills/evolution/core/engine.py:105-106` uses one LLM instance both to generate the variants and to score them, and the purpose-built independent judge channel `agent/skills/evolution/safety/judge.py:46` has **no construction site anywhere in the repo**. So the "inbreeding" critique holds for us too, in part. Already owned by `topic_08 #457`.
  2. **The yardstick itself can be rewritten by the proposer** — `agent/skills/evolution/pipeline/variant_generator.py:320-336` lets the same model co-evolve the `eval_cases` alongside the content, and `app/services/agent/confidence_approval_flow.py:140` writes them back into the skill record, so the **next** round's gate (`agent/skills/evolution/core/eval_regression.py:49`) measures with the rewritten cases. Within one round there is no drift (the original is the baseline); across rounds there is.
  3. **We do not do the "bots chatting in a group" form** — ours is single-agent skill evolution plus sub-agent orchestration (`toolkits/swarm/swarm_engine.py:24`); we neither copy that form nor claim to be better at "swarm intelligence".
- **One roadmap entry, extended rather than duplicated**: `topic_08 #457` (heterogeneous review and bounded convergence for skill evolution) — this round independently re-verified its core judgment and added the half it did not cover: **decoupling the reviewer is not enough; the yardstick has to be decoupled too**.
- **SSOT**: `temp-docs/materials/SKILL_EVOLUTION_ADVANTAGE.md` (main document of the skill & plugin axis, new 2026-10-22 section) · `temp-docs/roadmap/topic_08_skill_toolchain_roadmap.md` (#457) · `https://x.com/dingyi/status/2108395750128542180`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-22 · A Competitor Put the Agent Inside the Operating System: Herald OS's Permission Tiers, Zero-Model Fast Path and Plain-Language Crash Diagnosis · We Read Its Source Line by Line — and Corrected Its Own Architecture Diagram)

- **What triggered this**: a community post by @iamlukethedev (`https://x.com/iamlukethedev/status/2107765448343261506`, 2026-10-07 17:30) announcing **Herald OS** open source (MIT, `README.md:32` "Status: alpha (0.1)", `SECURITY.md:16` "Herald OS is pre-1.0"; the author states it is not affiliated with Nous Research); **114K views / 136 replies / 160 reposts / 1,092 likes / 1,320 bookmarks** — the community calls it the biggest community-built "agent lives in the OS" project. This round we extracted it with ChromeAgent :9410 + chrome-devtools MCP: **the full note tweet, all 191 replies (including the expanded "Show possible spam" section), the architecture diagram, and 10 frames sampled from the 147-second demo video**; we then pulled the source mirror locally (`external_reference_project/desktop/Herald-OS`) and read it file by file.
- **Verified competitor mechanisms (source level)**:
  1. **Permissions are a real four-tier enum, decided in code and never by the model** — `plugins/herald-os-bridge/bridge/permissions.py:28-32` (`Tier.READ` / `ACT` / `MUTATE` / `DESTRUCTIVE`); `tools.py:1112-1123` binds the tier **at the call site** (`remove_app` / `suspend` are `DESTRUCTIVE`); `permissions.py:45-49` with `:206-209` defines `BUILTIN_PROTECTED` (`~/.ssh`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/Library/Keychains`, `~/Library/Cookies`, `$HERMES_HOME/.env`, `auth.json`) and **refuses before any prompt**; `permissions.py:215-217` mints a **fresh rule key per call** for destructive actions, so "always allow" can never stick to them; `permissions.py:88-91` forces `destructive` back to `confirm` even when the policy says `allow`; `tools.py:1593-1594` guarantees the tier is never lowered. Users can tighten further via `permissions.yaml` in Settings > Privacy.
  2. **The audit log really lands on disk, and there is a user-visible view** — `plugins/herald-os-bridge/bridge/audit.py:33-54` appends one JSON line per tool call to `~/.hermes/herald-os/audit.jsonl` (tier / decision / ok / summary / error, secret fields `[redacted]`, 5 MB rotation); `tools.py:81-82` guarantees every call is recorded; the frontend renders an "Activity history" list at `apps/desktop/src/features/settings/sections/AgentsSection.tsx:480-531`. **Honest gap**: native OS commands the user or the voice layer triggers directly only land in a 50-entry in-memory HUD log — they are not persisted.
  3. **Plugins declare their capabilities before you enable them, and install switched off** — `apps/desktop/shared/plugins.ts:12` (`PluginPermission` is only `stats` / `notify` / `storage` / `run:<command.id>`), `:14-28` (the manifest must declare `permissions` and `hosts`), `:196-198` where `describePermissions()` returns the literal string `'Nothing beyond showing itself'` when the manifest asks for nothing, `:139-153` for the strict CSP; `apps/desktop/src/features/settings/sections/PluginsSection.tsx:129-134` renders that list item by item before enabling, `:54` states "From git — Installs it turned off; you see what it asks for before it runs."; `apps/desktop/electron/plugins/host.ts:176` records a git install as `(disabled)`, `:126` makes the grant exactly equal to the manifest; the renderer is an `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts">` (`apps/desktop/src/features/plugins/PluginFrame.tsx:100`).
  4. **The zero-model fast path is real, but it covers voice / command bar / CLI only** — `apps/desktop/src/lib/voice/intents.ts:1-4` ("match a transcript against the command registry's phrases **without a model round-trip**", unmatched utterances go to Hermes unchanged), `:128-130` (destructive commands are **deliberately excluded** from the fast path); `apps/desktop/src/store/voice.ts:304-317`; `apps/desktop/src/store/os-commands.ts:5-9`. **Typed chat always goes through the model** (`apps/desktop/src/store/chat.ts:175`, `prompt.submit`), and driving the OS through the `os_ui` tool is a model turn too.
  5. **Crashes are explained in plain words** — the 80-second mark of the demo video ("Crashes, explained in plain words") shows a four-part structure: what happened → will it happen again (a determinism verdict) → rule out environmental causes with concrete numbers (free RAM / free disk) and conclude "this is a code bug, not an environmental one" → where the fix is.
- **One "claimed ≠ delivered" this round corrected**: the architecture diagram lists `Watch folders` in the `Filesystem` domain, but **there is no user-facing folder-watch feature in the source** — every `fs.watch` in the repo is an internal detail (plugin folder hot reload `apps/desktop/electron/plugins/host.ts:375-380`, crash-report directory `apps/desktop/electron/crash/watch.ts:66`, Studio project folder refresh `apps/desktop/electron/ipc/fs.ts:901-910`, external-edit detection for open Office documents `apps/desktop/electron/office/file-watch.ts:8-13`), and its automation triggers are **cron schedules only** (`apps/desktop/src/features/automations/api.ts:54-56`); `Find by content` likewise **only holds on macOS** (Spotlight `kMDItemTextContent`, `plugins/herald-os-bridge/bridge/host/darwin.py:148`; on Linux it degrades to filename matching only — `plugins/herald-os-bridge/bridge/host/linux.py:397` says verbatim "Linux has no content index comparable to Spotlight", and there is no ripgrep anywhere in the repo). **We record this because it is exactly the kind of gap our own "never advertise undelivered capability" rule exists to prevent.**
- **Our side, checked against real code (nothing overclaimed)**:
  1. **Permission tiers are code constants** — `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/core/security/types.py:60-65` (`PermissionAction.ALLOW/ASK/DENY`) and `:107-120` (`DEFAULT_RULESET`); the deterministic evaluation entry point is `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/security/engine.py:281-300`, where `DENY` is a hard line the LLM reviewer cannot overturn.
  2. **Sensitive directories are hard forbidden zones** — `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/core/security/path/filesystem.py:59-71` (`~/.ssh`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/.gpg`, `~/.aws`, `~/.config/gcloud`, `~/.azure`, `~/.config`, `~/.docker`, `~/.kube` and two shell history files — 11 in total).
  3. **A plan → what changed → how to roll back handoff** — `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/middlewares/plan_confirm_middleware.py:49-63` (the plan is shown to the user first), shadow-Git snapshots isolated from the user's own repo (`myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/file_snapshot/shadow_git_store.py:97`), `POST /api/v1/approvals/{id}/rollback` (`myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/api/approvals/router.py:350-351`), and a one-click undo card at `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-frontend/src/components/approval/DestructiveUndoCard.tsx:51`.
  4. **External coding CLIs run as real child processes** — `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/toolkits/acp/runtime/cli_runtime.py:135-164` spawns a real process and parses NDJSON, mounted in production at `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/ai_agents/general_agent/external_agents.py:179-187` and `factory.py:228`.
  5. **Five event-style triggers, while the competitor has cron schedules only** — `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/toolkits/cron/triggers.py:38-45` (`TriggerKind`: EVENT / SYSTEM_EVENT / WEBHOOK / POLL / STREAM) wired in production at `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/channels/routing/router.py:678` → `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/toolkits/cron/engine/scheduler.py:297,321`.
  6. **The deterministic command table is already on the channel ingress path** — `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/channels/routing/command_defs.py:24-56` (25 command actions) + `command_registry.py:115-131` (pure lookup) + `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/channels/routing/router.py:664-668` (a match short-circuits; **the message never reaches the LLM**).
- **What we do not claim (honest gaps, all verified against real code)**:
  1. **Ordinary writes get no snapshot and no rollback** — only actions escalated to `ASK` take a snapshot (`myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/middlewares/approval/batch_processor.py:609-627`); a plain `file_write` resolves to the baseline `ALLOW`, produces no snapshot, no diff, and no one-click undo.
  2. **The Layer-1 capability fence is dead code in production** — `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/core/security/types.py:38-57` defines a deny-by-default `Capability` / `CapabilitySet`, but production always constructs `Capability("*","*")`, so it is never actually narrowed.
  3. **The dual-track audit collector has no production writer** — `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/services/security/dual_track_audit.py:34` is an in-memory ring buffer and `log_intent` / `complete_act` have no production caller. **We may say "approvals and actions have an audit ledger"; we may not say "the dual-track audit dashboard shows every action live".**
  4. **While an approval is pending, the user cannot queue or steer** — `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/services/agent/stream_session/stream_finalize.py:511` unregisters the steering token when the stream ends, so `POST /chats/{id}/steer` returns 404 during the approval `interrupt()`; only a single guidance text channel remains.
  5. **The plain-language crash diagnosis is not wired up** — the Chinese classifier at `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-frontend/src/components/features/diagnostics/stderrClassifier.ts:34` is only called from `useStderrDiagnosticStore.ts:27`, and that store is only called from tests; `ActionableDiagnosticSurfaceCard` has no render site; `POST /api/system/failure-triage`'s `TriageReport` (`myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/core/infra/health/failure_triage.py:63-80`) has no frontend consumer; **the system health score is hard-coded fake data ("98/100", `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/api/system_assistant/routes.py:64`) and must never be cited in any public material.**
  6. **No "capability list before enabling" UX for plugins** — the competitor's combination of manifest + disabled-by-default + itemised preview has no equivalent on our side. **Never conflate "we have a permission gate" with "we disclose capabilities before enabling" — they are not the same thing.**
  7. **No file-watch trigger, and no deterministic dispatch on the WebUI chat path** — the seven `TriggerKind` values contain no file-watch (`watchdog` only reloads the skill library, `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/backends/skills/watcher.py:21-23`); the deterministic command table only covers channel ingress.
- **Two new roadmap entries, neither delivered yet**: `topic_18 #178` (file-system watch trigger and the zero-model native command lane, **待分析 / not analyzed**) · `topic_10 #253` (plain-language runtime crash diagnosis and recurrence verdict wiring, **待分析 / not analyzed**).
- **Why the website was not changed this round**: every part we could verify as genuinely stronger on our side (the deterministic permission gate, plan → change → rollback, real ACP delegation) is **already stated on the website**; the three new findings are all **undelivered gaps**, and this project's "never advertise undelivered capability" rule forbids putting them on the website. The six core-capability blocks were left untouched.
- **SSOT**: `temp-docs/materials/SECURITY_ADVANTAGE.md` · `temp-docs/materials/AUTOMATION_CRON_ADVANTAGE.md` · `temp-docs/materials/OBSERVABILITY_ANALYTICS_ADVANTAGE.md` · `temp-docs/roadmap/topic_18_loop_engineering_goal_autonomous_scheduling_and_runtime_verification_roadmap.md` (#178) · `temp-docs/roadmap/topic_10_runtime_reliability_roadmap.md` (#253) · `external_reference_project/desktop/Herald-OS` · `https://x.com/iamlukethedev/status/2107765448343261506`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-22 · Can a Third-Party "Trust Score" Be Recomputed by Anyone? · Priors' recomputable record, published attack cost and capability degradation · Our honest gaps and one roadmap entry that is not delivered yet)

- **What triggered this**: a community post by @priors_trade (`https://x.com/priors_trade/status/2107819071836147885`, 2026-10-07 21:03; **3,477 views / 15 replies / 12 reposts / 90 likes / 4 bookmarks**) announcing its skill on ClawHub (`skills/priors`, "90+ downloads" per the post). We extracted the full post, **all 14 replies** and three frames of the 15-second demo with ChromeAgent :9410 + chrome-devtools MCP, then read its source mirror locally (`external_reference_project/claw/priors`, MIT) file by file.
- **Honest boundary first**: this is a **token promotion post** — the replies are almost entirely `$PRIORS` hype and memes, and reach is low; **nearly all of the mechanism information comes from its repository, not from the post**. What it builds is an **on-chain crypto credit pool** (unsecured USDG lending + ERC-8004 identity + x402 payments), which barely overlaps our office / coding / local-sandbox world. **We do not copy "put it on a chain"; we copy its evidence discipline.**
- **Four borrowable mechanisms (verified in source)**:
  1. **The credential can be recomputed by any third party, and the attack cost is published** — `README.md:12`: "Reviews are cheap to fake. Repaid debt isn't. … It is not unfakeable: **the score's own page lists what each attack costs and what still gets through**"; `docs/SCORE-v2.md:109` is an attack-by-attack table (`| attack | stopped by |`); `README.md:618`: "regression guard: **it runs the attack move for move and asserts containment. Revert the fix and it fails**" — **the attack itself is a regression guard**.
  2. **Capability degradation instead of a blackout** — `packages/mcp/README.md:166`: when the payment-state directory is not owner-only, "**the money tools refuse … and the read-only tools still answer**"; the hosted MCP is itself "**Read-only, no key**" (`skills/README.md:24-27`), and its write tools only build links after the owner confirms.
  3. **The skill ships its own "never fabricate / say it plainly when refused / never put the key on the command line" constraints** — `skills/priors/SKILL.md:25-27`: "⛔ Never invent a pool address … **when they refuse, say it plainly.**"; `:41-42`: "⛔ The key goes in the environment or `.env`, **never on the command line**"; `:114`: "`status` showing no loan proves nothing yet. The CLI refuses `borrow` … until the record is real."
  4. **Deployment facts are stated honestly** — the same deployment matrix says that with no deployment record there is nothing on-chain, and to **say so rather than pretend**.
- **Our side (checked against real code)**: four-tier skill trust plus user-controlled promotion/demotion (`app/core/skills/models.py:74,356`, `runtime_metadata.py:31,38`; frontend `SkillDetailSheetContent.tsx:255`); pre-install static scanning (26 categories / 113 patterns in `backends/skills/scanning/patterns.py`) with the finding count surfaced (`SkillCard.tsx:254-255`); "never fabricate" enforced by execution-layer gates; and a working precedent for listing unverified items explicitly (the hardware capability matrix).
- **What we do not claim (honest gaps)**:
  1. **Every third-party trust signal we expose is self-attested by the platform** — scan results, stars, download counts and user-granted TRUSTED are all in our own or the marketplace's terms; **none of them can be recomputed by an independent third party**, so a user seeing "scanned / 0 findings" cannot re-derive it.
  2. **No published attack-cost / residual-attack-surface table** — we report a finding count, not what the scan does and does not cover.
  3. **No capability-level degradation when admitting an untrusted MCP** — today an unusable tool fails the run, rather than "sensitive tool refused, read-only tool still answers".
  4. **No mandatory "never fabricate / say it plainly / never put the key on the command line" clause in the skill authoring contract** — our execution-layer gates are the backstop, but the **skill text itself** carries no such requirement.
- **One new roadmap entry, not delivered yet**: `topic_08 #479` (third-party credential recomputability and capability degradation discipline suite, **not analyzed**).
- **SSOT**: `temp-docs/materials/SKILL_EVOLUTION_ADVANTAGE.md` (main document of the skill & plugin axis, new 2026-10-22 section) · `temp-docs/roadmap/topic_08_skill_toolchain_roadmap.md` (#479) · `external_reference_project/claw/priors` · `https://x.com/priors_trade/status/2107819071836147885`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-22 · A Competitor Hooked Its Team Agent to X to Trigger Work · Route by Responsibility and Let People Decline · Eleven community critiques carry more signal than the post itself, plus our seven honest gaps and one roadmap entry that is not delivered yet)

- **What triggered this**: X @steipete (OpenClaw founder) `https://x.com/steipete/status/2107697554448421160` (2026-10-07 17:00; **61K views / 74 replies / 25 reposts / 306 likes / 75 bookmarks**), quoting a PR-review session opened by their team bot @roboclawbot (PR #166379). This round we extracted the full post and **60+ replies** with ChromeAgent :9410 + chrome-devtools MCP.
- **What the competitor does (three things wired together)**: ① **X (Twitter) as the work-trigger ingress**; ② **unassigned sessions open to anyone, first come first claw**; ③ **the agent finds who last worked on the related code and pings that person on the team server**; the whole thing was "a prompt and the team server extended itself since plugins are now hot reloadable".
- **What the community objected to (worth more than the post)**: @JMkernt — "this is basically **self-serve paging** and I love it. The interesting failure mode is **the agent pinging the wrong person at 3am and waking up someone who hasn't touched that code in a year**"; @leohuanfang — "does it wait for them to claim it **or reassign after a timeout**?"; @mynameisdesmond — "how do you handle two people grabbing the same session? **A clear claim and release mechanism**"; @jjfleagle — "**letting people decline with a reason** could teach the team claw the difference between **who changed the code and who owns it**"; @jeffbruchado — "**the last person to touch a file shouldn't automatically become its owner**"; @Atlas_fomo — "pinging the last author is **one git log lookup, and it beats a round-robin queue because that person already has the context loaded**. The risk is the same name getting pinged for every session on a hot file"; @sakshjn — "hope it respects **.git-blame-ignore-revs**"; @emonuxui — "unassigned sessions turn the backlog into **a pull system**"; @wlodarczykacper — "now that work comes in from X, **can a session triggered from X write plugins too? Public posts are untrusted input** 🙂".
- **Our side (checked against real code)**: **claiming is already atomic** (`app/core/kanban/adapters/sqlalchemy_store.py:730-758`, `claim_task`: `READY → RUNNING` plus `worker_id` in metadata); **heartbeat-timeout zombie detection exists** (same file `:376-378`, `:825-828`); **`git blame` is already runnable** (`toolkits/code_execution/security/safe_command_configs/git.py:183`); **the write fence for untrusted ingress already exists** (read-only allow-list, topic_03 #423).
- **What we do not claim (honest gaps)**:
  1. **No first-class "last modifier" fact** — no server-side module produces a structured "who last touched this code" result; the model can only run blame and read free text.
  2. **No release and no decline-with-reason** — `grep "release_claim\|reassign"` returns nothing.
  3. **No timeout reassignment** — a heartbeat timeout only marks the task as a zombie candidate; it never returns to the pool or moves to another person.
  4. **`.git-blame-ignore-revs` is not respected** — bulk-rewrite commits would point the signal at one person.
  5. **No decay on the routing signal and no do-not-disturb guard** — "the same three people get pinged for everything" holds on our side too.
  6. **Public channels are not a work-trigger ingress for us** — we do X search and evidence collection, not dispatch; and an X-triggered session is untrusted input, so the write boundary must be fixed first (we do **not** copy "one X sentence can change the server").
  7. **The queue is still push-based** — there is no "unassigned, anyone may grab" pull semantics.
  8. **We do not copy "the server extends itself"** — our model is a user-owned sandbox with three isomorphic surfaces, not runtime self-extension.
- **One new roadmap entry, not delivered yet**: `topic_20 #64` (last-touch ownership routing and declinable claim dispatch suite, **not analyzed**).
- **SSOT**: `temp-docs/materials/GIT_PR_DELIVERY_ADVANTAGE.md` (existing Git-delivery document, new 2026-10-22 section) · `temp-docs/roadmap/topic_20_coding_agent_and_software_engineering_roadmap.md` (#64) · `https://x.com/steipete/status/2107697554448421160`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-22 · Progressive disclosure finally has comparable numbers: 1.2k index, 3.1k–5.2k body, unload after ship · The comments point out the failure moved to "which skill gets picked" · We already have "loaded is not the same as used" attribution, but no same-yardstick public numbers, no unload, and no ambiguity check)

- **What triggered this**: X @vibelancer (`https://x.com/vibelancer/status/2107773037739204914`, 2026-10-07) published **SKILL FORGE** — Claude Skills as a factory floor: 12 SKILL.md cartridges on a shelf, one 200k-context agent core, four prompts each carrying one skill, **loaded only when the job actually asks for it**. We extracted the full post, **14 replies** and demo-video frames with ChromeAgent :9410 + chrome-devtools MCP.
- **The three comparable numbers the competitor gives**:
  1. **Index always resident**: "INDEXER drone: reads only the YAML frontmatter, name + description · **12 × ~100 tokens = 1.2k always in context** · scans the whole shelf in 1.05s";
  2. **Body loaded on demand**: "base context **8.4k** · the SKILL.md body lands on plug: **pptx 4.8k, xlsx 3.9k, docx 5.2k, pdf 3.1k**";
  3. **Unload after ship**: "**unloads after ship, budget drops back to ~9.8k**", the whole chain being **PROMPT → MATCH → LOAD → RUN → SHIP → UNLOAD** — the point being not to stuff 17k tokens of four manuals into every request.
- **What the comments flagged (the valuable part)**: @asmirkn — "i'd want **a log of which cartridge it picked**. no backend means when the output is wrong you can't tell if the skill was bad or **the wrong one got loaded**"; @lunkertw — "**what decides whether it works is the one-line description on each skill**: if the agent can't tell when to load it, 12 cartridges become 12 guesses"; @chaka_kuka — "it keeps the active context lean, **but it moves the failure point into routing** … **the agent needs an ambiguity check before execution**"; @kartikb753 — "the indexer routes on the 100 token frontmatter alone so a vague one loads the wrong 5k body for nothing"; @BoazWith (a measurement we can reuse) — "three orchestrator agent files went from **651 / 533 / 844 lines to 37 / 34 / 38** once the detail moved into reference files that only load when a step needs them".
- **Our side (checked against real code)**: **"loaded" is explicitly distinguished from "used"** in our statistics — `backends/skills/types/types_usage.py:101` ("Uses whose outcome was observable (**selections without tool calls and ambiguous runs are not**)") and `backends/skills/stats_collector.py:88` ("the skill was only loaded, or several loaded skills made the owner ambiguous") — **exactly @asmirkn's ask**; the per-turn selected-skill count is persisted and aggregable (`app/database/models/turn_capability_metric.py:32` `selected_skill_count`, `app/api/statistics/turn_capability.py:93` `avg_selected_skill_count`); when two skills look alike you can **pick by hand** (session-level skill selection plus `MigrationWizardDrawer.tsx:33`) instead of gambling on one high score; four-tier progressive disclosure and a Turn-1 tool/prompt token budget CI gate already exist.
- **What we do not claim (honest gaps)**:
  1. **No same-yardstick public numbers** — the competitor publishes index/body/restored; we publish none, so "who is cheaper" can only be an adjective today.
  2. **No skill-level "unload after ship"** — `grep "unload\|evict_skill\|drop_skill"` returns nothing in the skill or context-compaction code; we only compress afterwards.
  3. **No ambiguity check before execution** — `grep "ambigu\|similar_desc\|description_similarity"` returns nothing; our guard is the manual path, not an automatic "these two look alike, ask first".
  4. **Which skill got picked is recorded as a count, not an identity** — the per-task identity the community asks for is not yet a column in our session statistics.
  5. **We do not pre-judge "who is cheaper"** until we have same-yardstick measurements of our own.
- **One new roadmap entry, not delivered yet**: `topic_08 #480` (skill progressive-disclosure load/unload closure and selection traceability suite, **not analyzed**).
- **SSOT**: `temp-docs/materials/SKILL_EVOLUTION_ADVANTAGE.md` (main document of the skill & plugin axis, new 2026-10-22 section) · `temp-docs/roadmap/topic_08_skill_toolchain_roadmap.md` (#480) · `https://x.com/vibelancer/status/2107773037739204914`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-22 · Where a memory should live, how you fix it, and whether you can see it in one place: Hermes' five memory tips plus the community's follow-ups · Four of our primitives are already on par with the competitor; the real gaps are the placement rule, one unified timeline, conflict detection, and how we state the privacy boundary)

- **What triggered this**: X @witcheer (Nous community member) "memory in Hermes Agent, here are five tips" (`https://x.com/witcheer/status/2107866669041283548`, 2026-10-08 12:12; **9,317 views / 10 replies / 8 reposts / 192 likes / 255 bookmarks**). We extracted the full post and **10 replies** with ChromeAgent :9410 + chrome-devtools MCP.
- **The competitor's five tips (directly actionable)**:
  1. **Native memory is on by default** — notes live in `~/.hermes/memories/MEMORY.md`, a profile of you in `USER.md`, and **both are loaded at the start of every session**; a memory provider runs alongside it to add semantic search, knowledge graphs and automatic fact extraction (`hermes memory setup` / `status`).
  2. **Memory can stay completely local** — the memory files live on your disk and **every past chat is stored in `~/.hermes/state.db`**, which the agent can search.
  3. **An off-the-shelf wiki** — the `llm-wiki` skill **ships with Hermes Agent** and maintains a knowledge base as interlinked markdown in `~/wiki`, where **the folder works as an Obsidian vault**.
  4. **Facts to memory, steps to skills (the most quotable rule)** — "memory is for facts the agent needs in every session, so they sit in its prompt from the start. a skill loads only when it's relevant, which often makes it the better home for what a recurring task needs".
  5. **You can see, fix and approve** — `hermes journey` shows **memory entries and learned skills on one timeline**, `journey edit` / `delete` corrects them, and `memory: write_approval: true` in `config.yaml` makes **every save wait for approval**.
- **What the community added (the author didn't cover it)**: @hypermageia — "I often run into issues where an agent will sometimes **store memory in the wrong place or record conflicting** entries"; @humblyonline (extending tip 4) — "sometimes the answer is **the fact should go in a database, which is wired up with a CLI or MCP the agent knows how to operate**"; @juliusg — "if I run a local LLM and a Nous cloud agent on the same machine, **the cloud agent can read the local's work — should I still consider any of that private?**"
- **Our side (checked against real code)**: **the plain-text memory face is on par** (`app/schemas/profile_notes.py:59-60,68-69` — `user_content` / `memory_content`, described as "Updated USER.md content" / "Updated MEMORY.md content"; `app/schemas/markdown_chunker.py:58` — `source_path` defaults to `"MEMORY.md"`); **write approval already exists** (`app/schemas/memory/command_center.py:657` "Candidate memory awaiting user approval or graduation", `app/schemas/memory/crud.py:93` "Pending memory item awaiting user approval"); **Obsidian-friendly export exists** (`app/api/wiki/router.py:2905` `export_wiki_vault`, `:2920` `build_wiki_export_zip`); **the memory-side timeline plus correction chain is already delivered** (`temp-docs/materials/MEMORY_ADVANTAGE.md:187`); pluggable storage plus semantic search / knowledge graph / automatic fact extraction (`MEMORY_ADVANTAGE.md:168`).
- **What we do not claim (honest gaps)**: ① **no single rule for "where does this belong"** — memory, skills, tools and the knowledge base each have mechanisms, but there is no unified placement decision before a write; ② **memories and skills are not on one timeline** — skill-evolution review lives in a separate panel; ③ **no placement-correctness or conflict check before writing**; ④ **"local" is not "private" must be stated harder** — visibility between cloud-sandbox artifacts and on-machine artifacts has to be spelled out, not just "stored locally".
- **One new roadmap entry, not delivered yet**: `topic_01 #211` (memory·skill·artifact placement rule and unified timeline suite, **not analyzed**).
- **SSOT**: `temp-docs/materials/MEMORY_ADVANTAGE.md` (main document of the memory & knowledge axis, new 2026-10-22 section) · `temp-docs/roadmap/topic_01_memory_roadmap.md` (#211) · `https://x.com/witcheer/status/2107866669041283548`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-22 · A competitor turned "just talk to your coding agent, it edits the video" into a 65K-star project · The comments ask the three questions that matter: should it stop when a step fails, can the footage be used commercially, and do you have to re-roll the whole cut for one change · Our three strengths and one honest gap)

- **What triggered this**: X @0xGky "an open-source project taking GitHub by storm — OpenMontage" (`https://x.com/0xGky/status/2108006966974972326`, 2026-10-08 09:30; **27K views / 20 replies / 77 reposts / 371 likes / 473 bookmarks**). We extracted the full post and **23 replies** with ChromeAgent :9410 + chrome-devtools MCP and pulled the source mirror locally (`external_reference_project/harness/OpenMontage`, **AGPL-3.0**).
- **What it does**: you talk to Claude Code / Cursor in plain language and it **writes the script, finds footage, does the voiceover, burns in subtitles and assembles the final cut**; it can also **reverse-engineer a hit video** (script rhythm, shot keyframes, style) and hand you two or three different directions.
- **⚠️ Scale correction (measured in the mirror vs the post's claim)**: the mirror contains **139** `SKILL.md` files, **265** tool scripts and **4** pipeline directories; the post claims "12 pipelines, 100+ tools, 700+ agent skills". **These do not match — we quote our measured numbers with the snapshot date.**
- **The three questions the comments raise (worth more than the post)**: @llloooo24 (615 likes) — "**I want to see the time it failed. When the footage can't be found or the voice doesn't match the picture, does it stop and ask, or does it still hand over a complete video? Delivering homework is easy. Knowing which step should not continue is what makes it a usable pipeline.**"; @hinanwang (698 likes) — "**footage rights**: AI-found footage and generated BGM — **could this blow up commercially?** Until that's spelled out I wouldn't take client work with it"; @linyiq (421 likes) — "the worst thing about tools like this is '**the cut is fast but one change means rolling the dice again**' … you need to swap a shot, change the BGM, lock a subtitle **without re-running the whole video**, and the **timeline and asset references** it produces need to open in the editor you already use".
- **Our side (checked against real code)**: **completion is decided by physical facts** — the middleware chain at `agent/_internals/_agent_build.py:98-107` includes `CompletionGuard()` alongside `ToolApprovalMiddleware` — so **"stop when a step fails" is an existing mechanism on our side, and still an open question on theirs**; **what we deliver is an editable intermediate artifact** (`agent/artifacts/vault.py:35-45,42` — `VAULT_PREFIX` and `sha256_hash`; `core/artifacts/manifest.py:110-140` — `DeliverableItem` with `version_id` / `vault_uri`; incremental diffs surface via `fileDiffEvents.ts:31`); **licensing freedom**: both of our repos are MIT, the competitor is AGPL-3.0.
- **What we do not claim (honest gaps)**: ① **no "asset provenance and license lineage"** — searching `copyright` / `attribution` / `license` / `授权` / `版权` under `app/api/artifacts` and `app/services/artifacts` returns **nothing**; ② **we have no system-level video pipeline** (topic_16 entries 12 / 33 / 35 are all **not analyzed** and must not be presented as shipped); ③ **we do not compete on scale** — we compete on "every one of them passes the security and evidence gate"; ④ **we do not quote their cost number** ("$1.33 for 60 seconds" is their demo figure).
- **One new roadmap entry, not delivered yet**: `topic_16 #411` (media artifact provenance and license lineage suite, **not analyzed**).
- **SSOT**: `temp-docs/materials/AI_CREATION_WORKFLOW_AND_MODEL_ORCHESTRATION_ADVANTAGE.md` (main document of the creation & multimodal axis, new 2026-10-22 section) · `temp-docs/roadmap/topic_16_office_agent_specialty_suites_and_deliverable_bundle_roadmap.md` (#411) · `external_reference_project/harness/OpenMontage`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-22 · Official certification courses now cover "Agent Skills" and "subagents" · The certificate is a side effect; **the order of the path** is what matters · Our "teach the AI to write skills" is already a closed loop and already stated on the website — what's missing is a capability path for users)

- **What triggered this**: X @Amelia_With_Ai (Amelia Brooks) "Claude is offering 18 official AI courses with certificates. And it's 100% free" (`https://x.com/Amelia_With_Ai/status/2108142676881362977`, 2026-10-08 18:29; **2,292 views / 19 replies / 43 reposts / 75 likes / 37 bookmarks**) — Anthropic Academy (`anthropic.skilljar.com`), 18 free courses, including #5 "Introduction to Agent Skills", #18 "Introduction to subagents", #8/#9 on MCP and #7 "Claude Code in Action". We extracted the full post and **17 replies** with ChromeAgent :9410 + chrome-devtools MCP.
- **The signal**: skills, subagents and MCP have entered the **top level of official certification courses** (not community tutorials, but courses with certificates) → skills move from a hobby to a **teachable, testable, résumé-able** professional skill. This is the **second independent confirmation** of the trend already recorded in our roadmap (topic_08: "official vendors treat skills as first-class citizens").
- **The only informative reply (@AleksejAros, 42 likes)**: "18 free courses is a lot of homework, but Claude 101 then the Cowork workflows one is a sensible order. **Certificates I'd treat as a side effect.**" → **the certificate is a side effect; the order of the path is what matters.**
- **The more borrowable part of the ecosystem**: **"teach an AI to write skills" has itself become an installable skill** — the mirror contains at least four `skill-creator` copies (`external_reference_project/claw/anthropics-skills/skills/skill-creator`, `openclaw/skills/skill-creator`, `LobsterAI/SKILLs/skill-creator`, `awesome-claude-skills/skill-creator`).
- **Our side (checked against real code)**: **we do a whole extra loop beyond a generic `skill-creator`** — entry point `myrm-agent-frontend/src/lib/skills/composeLearnSlashMessage.ts:51` (builds the `/learn` message) plus the website's own wording "Settings learning wizard: directory + URL + description + five scenario chips → one click `/learn` (opens a chat) → draft review → `[use skill]` invocation guide"; distillation and gating `myrm-agent-harness/.../agent/skills/evolution/core/engine.py` (evolution engine) + `eval_regression.py` (regression assertions after evolution) + `myrm-agent-server/app/services/skills/evolution_review/actions.py` (human review actions); the gate itself is pre-install static scanning (26 categories / 113 patterns) + four-tier skill trust + user-controlled promotion and revocation. **⇒ this advantage is already stated honestly on our website; no new marketing is needed this round.**
- **What we do not claim (honest gaps)**: ① **no user-facing "capability path / learning map"** — our 134 built-in skills, 26 scan categories, 26 channels and four-tier progressive disclosure are presented **flat**; the competitor at least offers **an ordered on-ramp** (Claude 101 → Cowork), and @AleksejAros's point ("what's missing is order, not a certificate") lands exactly here. ② **No new roadmap entry this round**: this is a **product shape** (a capability map / progressive learning path), not a runtime mechanism, and it sits next to the same-axis document `SKILL_MARKET_AND_ADOPTION_ADVANTAGE.md` — **to avoid duplicating work inside one axis we only register the need and the current state**; that document will carry it once the shape is settled.
- **SSOT**: `temp-docs/materials/SKILL_EVOLUTION_ADVANTAGE.md` (main document of the skill & plugin axis, new 2026-10-22 section) · `temp-docs/materials/SKILL_MARKET_AND_ADOPTION_ADVANTAGE.md` (the single place that carries it) · `https://x.com/Amelia_With_Ai/status/2108142676881362977`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-22 · A competitor sets two gates before an agent may spend your money: the facts are real, and the agent is really yours · One comment says trust needs proof, not promises · We add a third leg — "what if it buys the wrong thing" — without quoting their forecast numbers or doing on-chain identity)

- **What triggered this**: X @billions_ntwk "How to let your OpenClaw agent spend your money safely (full guide)" (`https://x.com/billions_ntwk/status/2108212989585154475`, 2026-10-08 23:08; **30K views / 420 replies / 305 reposts / 354 likes**), with @Verona_dev. We extracted the full post and **132 replies** with ChromeAgent :9410 + chrome-devtools MCP.
- **The competitor's two gates**: ① "**What it's acting on is real.** The price, the seller, the ticket: none of it can be fake." — Verona checks facts with **zero-knowledge proofs** (claims 5M+ users and 70M+ verified interactions); ② "**The agent is really yours.** It has to be clear who the agent buys for, and who answers if it makes a mistake." — Billions gives each agent an ID card bound to a verified real person (claims 2.5M+ verified users and 25,000+ agents carrying an ID).
- **Their own industry read**: "So far, most of the effort has gone into making agents smarter. **Much less has gone into making sure they're safe to hand your wallet to.**"
- **The only informative reply (@INIODU 💛, 112 likes)**: "**trust needs proof not promises** — appreciate the guide!" → **trust needs proof, not promises.**
- **Our side (checked against real code)**: **the ownership half has a locally verifiable counterpart** — the unified approval ledger `approvals` (`myrm-agent-server/app/database/models/approval.py:15-30`: `agent_id` / `chat_id` / `thread_id` / `action_type` / `reason` / status and expiry) + a per-action audit trail (`myrm-agent-harness/.../core/security/audit.py:191-200` `record_decision`) + **the agent's capability scope limited by signed tokens** (`core/security/ocap/token.py:49-53` `sign_capability_handle`: HMAC-SHA256 signature, constant-time verification, attenuable); **the facts half is traceable rather than cryptographic** (grounded claims / publish compliance gate / fact arbitration back to primary sources / full-chain evidence tracing); **and "what if it buys the wrong thing" is the one leg we have that they don't** — plan review → incremental diff → shadow snapshot → one-click rollback.
- **What we do not claim (honest boundaries)**: ① **we do not quote their forecast numbers** (the McKinsey $3–5T and Deloitte $40B figures are **third-party projections**, not measured capability); ② **we do not do ZKP or on-chain identity** — our path is a locally verifiable, offline-capable evidence chain plus approval ownership; ③ **we have no "identity card a third party can independently verify"** — if your business strictly requires the counterparty to verify you, that is their turf and we will not pretend otherwise; ④ **no new roadmap entry this round** — the gap would require an on-chain or third-party service, which conflicts with our local-self-hosting route; the shape-level needs already live in `topic_20 #64` (responsibility routing and declinable claims) and `topic_01 #211` (placement rule and unified timeline).
- **SSOT**: `temp-docs/materials/COMMERCE_AGENT_TRANSACTIONAL_ADVANTAGE.md` (main document of the commerce & organisation axis, new section eight, 2026-10-22) · `temp-docs/materials/SECURITY_ADVANTAGE.md` (plan → change → rollback handoff) · `https://x.com/billions_ntwk/status/2108212989585154475`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-22 · A competitor ships a 24-item install directory, and one comment says "installed 24, used 3" · Installed is not used: we make that measurable — but we do not yet have keyword-triggered skills)

- **What triggered this**: X @charliejhills "These are the 24 installs worth adding" (`https://x.com/charliejhills/status/2108222962306548005`, 2026-10-08 23:48; **27K views / 27 replies / 26 reposts / 251 likes / 323 bookmarks**) — 8 plugins, 8 on-demand skills, 8 MCP servers. We extracted the full post and **22 replies** with ChromeAgent :9410 + chrome-devtools MCP.
- **Checkable facts**: plugins `claude-skills` (claims **263+** skills), `marketingskills` (40 growth tools at once), `gstack` (23 dev tools in one install); skills `frontend-design` / `skill-creator` / `hyperframes` / `mcp-builder` / `claude-seo` / `find-skills` / `humanizer` / `caveman`, with the claim "**One keyword fires each one from any chat.**"; MCP servers Notion / Slack / Higgsfield / Zapier (9,000+ apps) / `agent-browser` (browser on fewer tokens) / Perplexity / Granola / Kondo. The post's own thesis: "**The install is the easy part. Chaining them is the win.**"
- **The sharpest reply (@squirrelprompts, 65 likes)**: "Installed 3. The other 21 went wherever my buried acorns go. **If you could only keep one, which one earns the spot?**" → **24 installed, 3 used, 21 rotting: the install count is not the capability.** (The author adds, 170 likes: "Exactly the magic happens between the tools.")
- **Our side (checked against real code)**: **"installed is not used" is measurable on our side** — the three-state attribution (`backends/skills/types/types_usage.py:101` "selections without tool calls and ambiguous runs are not"; `backends/skills/stats_collector.py:88` "the skill was only loaded, or several loaded skills made the owner ambiguous"); **we consume the same ecosystem** (`agent/skills/market/sources/clawhub.py:1-4`); we ship **134 `SKILL.md`** of our own.
- **What we do not claim (honest gaps)**: **we have no deterministic keyword → skill trigger table** — `backends/skills/market_protocols.py:40`'s `keywords` is a *market-source metadata field*, not a runtime trigger rule; our implicit triggering relies on model judgement plus session-level skill selection. **We do not compete on scale** (their 263+ is a community plugin pool, our 134 are built in — different units).
- **No new roadmap entry this round**: keyword triggering and usage-rate measurement are folded into the addendum on `topic_08 #480` — **no duplicate entry inside the same axis**.
- **SSOT**: `temp-docs/materials/SKILL_EVOLUTION_ADVANTAGE.md` (main document of the skill & plugin axis, 2026-10-22 addendum) · `temp-docs/roadmap/topic_08_skill_toolchain_roadmap.md` (#480 with addendum) · `https://x.com/charliejhills/status/2108222962306548005`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-21 · Hermes Gadget SDK: How a Competitor Put an Agent Into Every Device · Four Verified Mechanisms, Our Honest Gap, and Two Roadmap Entries That Are Not Yet Delivered)

- **What triggered this**: a community post by @adolandev (`https://x.com/adolandev/status/2107813150686888407`) — "Three days ago, Hermes Gadget was an open SDK and a demo. Then the community got hold of it -- shoutout @NousResearch. LilyGO watches. AIPI Lite. Desk gadgets. Old Android phones. People are getting Hermes running on all kinds of devices and sharing their work." We pulled the SDK repository (`external_reference_project/claw/hermes-gadget-sdk`, MIT, v0.2.0) and read it file by file. Note: it is a **third-party Hermes plugin, not a NousResearch product**.
- **Four verified mechanisms**:
  1. **One device core, three runtimes (MCU / Linux / desktop)** — `docs/simulator.md:5` "runs the **production device core** (`firmware/core`) as a shared library inside a Python host"; `:23` "A bug reproduced in the simulator is therefore a bug in the firmware."; `python/hermes_gadget/sim/native.py:1-5` (only the drivers are replaced, the state machine is not); `pyproject.toml:35-39` (the dev server and the tests load **the exact code the gateway loads**).
  2. **Develop, try and accept without hardware** — a stand-in gateway (`docs/simulator.md:42`), a desktop simulator, 14 board profiles (`python/hermes_gadget/sim/runner.py:44`), and a headless script DSL that asserts on device screen states (`docs/simulator.md:104-120`, screen states enumerated at `:125`).
  3. **A per-model hardware verification matrix with exactly two support labels** — `docs/hardware-validation.md:7` ("A passing build alone does not establish that a microphone, power circuit, or display works on a device"), 19 model rows each carrying its own verification column (`:11-31`; the flagship breadboard's cell reads "CI build; physical report not recorded"), the two labels Experimental / Hardware verified (`:236-239`), and the rules "A report for one model or revision does not verify another" and "Update this page in the same pull request that adds a port or changes its verified capabilities" (`:241`).
  4. **A community device zoo, each entry with its own verified / unverified list** — LilyGO T-Display-S3 (`:29`), Xorigin AIPI Lite (`:24`), Elecrow CrowPanel Advanced P4 (`:23`), Espressif ESP-VoCat (`:22`), Spotpear 1.28-BOX (`:25`), Waveshare AMOLED 1.75 / 1.8 / 2.06 / 2.16 (`:16-19`).
- **Our side, checked against real code (nothing overclaimed)**:
  1. **No device channel yet** — `app/channels/providers/` holds 24 IM / email / voice implementations and zero device channels; the device channel itself is still roadmap entry `topic_19 #60` (**待分析 / not delivered**).
  2. **No virtual twin yet** — searching `myrm-agent` for `esp32` / `gadget` returns nothing; the only module named `simulator` is the memory-recall simulator (`app/services/memory/recall_simulator_service.py`). New entry `topic_19 #67` (**待分析**).
  3. **No capability verification matrix yet** — platform switches are inlined ad hoc (`myrm-agent-desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/screen_lock.rs:82`), while the docs state platform capabilities uniformly (`docs/getting-started/desktop-app.mdx:30,44,53`) without any verification status, even though our own roadmap already carries "未验证：Windows 真机" tails. New entry `topic_19 #68` (**待分析**).
  4. **What we already ship, the part that needs no hardware** — voice barge-in (`app/api/voice/agent_bridge.py:187-206`), live meeting notes (`app/services/meeting_notes/live_notes.py:42,59,183`), the mobile command deck (`app/remote_access/mobile_hub_payload.py:20,28,45` plus `components/features/mobile/`), and one-click web clipping into the knowledge base (`app/services/wiki/clip/runner.py:37-45,82`).
- **What we are *not* claiming**: the peripheral state bus, the local open Vault, loopback meeting recording, the personal hotword lexicon and YoooClaw asset migration are **not implemented** — see the "未交付清单（禁止宣传）" table in `temp-docs/materials/PHYSICAL_COMPANION_AND_CONTEXT_ASSETS_ADVANTAGE.md`.

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-21 · Nous Research Confirms Series B · Community Sentiment Measured: The Funding Narrative Gets Mocked, "Enterprise + Consumer" Split Gets Criticized, and What Users Actually Want Is "Web With Desktop Capabilities That Can Reach Remote Machines")

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-21 · How the skills ecosystem actually ranks: GitHub stars drift 43% in a month and reorder the list · Only real installs and reputation can be a trustworthy ranking signal)

- **The ecosystem's current ranking signal and its measured drift (benchmarking X post @I_am_Aiabir, "Top 10 Hermes skills ranked by Github stars")**: the community currently uses GitHub stars as a proxy for skill quality — and that proxy is **not stable**.
  1. **Image figures (skillleaderboard.com, data through 2026-09-20)**: superpowers 289k · ponytail 143k · graphify 120k · caveman 107k · Understand-Anything 83.4k · last30days 62.4k · i-have-adhd 49.1k · agentic-awesome-skills 46.6k · scientific-agent-skills 45.7k · diagram-design 41.5k;
  2. **Same-source GitHub API measurement (2026-10-21)**: 302,914 / 170,840 / 131,042 / 114,028 / 87,395 / — / 58,109 / 48,249 / 53,497 / 59,330 → **diagram-design moved from #10 (41.5k) to roughly #6-7 (59,330, +43% in a month)** and scientific-agent-skills / agentic-awesome-skills swapped positions;
  3. **Conclusion**: stars can be gamed *and* drift with attention, so they cannot decide "which skill is worth installing"; a trustworthy ranking must come from **real active installs + retention + audit-clean records** (ours: `topic_08 #279④`, **planned — must not be advertised as shipped before delivery**);
  4. **A second structural fact**: the star top ten is **almost entirely coding-oriented** (methodology framework / codebase knowledge graph / token-saving voice / output style / cross-platform research / diagram aesthetics), while we are a **GUI general-office and personal agent** → our curation battlefield is **not the same quadrant** as the star list, and the most-starred community skills must not become our default selection;
  5. **Four borrowable mechanics (all sources already cloned locally under `external_reference_project/harness/`)**: ① **reuse-first discipline** (`ponytail` `AGENTS.md`: "Before you write", "The smallest complete change") — we have the skill market, `/learn` skill distillation and code/knowledge graphs, but **no default "look for an existing solution first" behaviour**; ② **codebase/knowledge base → interactive knowledge graph** (`Understand-Anything`: a multi-agent pipeline turns files/functions/classes/dependencies into nodes with plain-English summaries and guided tours) — we have code graphs and memory knowledge graphs; **node-level summaries and tours** are the gap; ③ **executable visual-accessibility parameters for artifacts** (`diagram-design`: one accent color / 1px hairlines / no shadows / 4px grid / automatic contrast checks / accessible by default / offline fonts / one self-contained HTML+SVG file, and an honest note that the output cannot be diffed → it is for "the diagram you are going to publish") — already folded into `topic_14 #255`; ④ **domain-bundled skill packs with examples and security reports shipped alongside** (`scientific-agent-skills`: per-scientific-domain directories plus examples / security report / sponsor transparency) — we already have 14 filters and trust tiers; bundling and per-pack transparency are the additions;
  6. **Link check**: the GitHub paths in the post are **truncated by the author** (e.g. `github.com/obra/superpowe`) and cannot be opened directly → full repo names and stars were resolved through the GitHub API; our local `Understand-Anything` is the `Lum1104` fork while the post points at upstream `Egonex-AI/Understand-Anything`;
  7. **SSOT:** `temp-docs/materials/SKILL_MARKET_AND_ADOPTION_ADVANTAGE.md` ("How the community ranks skills" section) · `external_reference_project/harness/{ponytail,Understand-Anything,diagram-design,scientific-agent-skills}` · `https://api.github.com/repos/NousResearch/hermes-agent`

On OpenRouter (thread-reported, **not independently verified**): 51T+ tokens cumulative, ~500 models, ranked first in Productivity / Coding / Personal & CLI Agents, ~13.9T tokens per week (+11%).
