## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-21 · Private Regression Assets & Evaluation Credibility: Turning Your Own Pitfalls Into Guardrails · Eliminating Silent Agent Regressions After a Model or Prompt Swap — And Public Benchmarks That Cannot Test Your Business Pitfalls)

- **Private Regression Assets & Evaluation Credibility Suite (Benchmarking Hermes / OpenClaw / Codex / DeepSeek-Harness / Pi / OpenCode on "does a config change regress the agent, and is there a guardrail against it"):** Competitors generally leave quality to developer-only offline scripts or public datasets — after a model swap you regress by hand. Anthropic's `claude-api` skill now ships `build-eval` / `hillclimb` plus a full eval-health checklist, but it is a **CLI developer tool that only launched in 2026-09**: it runs inside Claude Code, has no GUI, and never captures your private cases. Myrm turns this into a **product capability**:
  1. **Private cases are assets (`app/core/eval/capture.py` → `.myrm/eval_datasets/`)**: right-click any solved task in a chat and "distill it into a test case"; base64 image payloads are replaced with placeholder metadata before write (`app/core/eval/capture.py:32-45`); 2.4MB → 3.8KB, editor opens instantly; rerun with one click before switching models, editing prompts or upgrading skills — per-case pass rate, latency and tokens;
  2. **Four-layer anti-contamination (`eval/canary.py` · `app/core/eval/wb_bench/workspace.py:83-87` · `eval/contamination.py` · `eval/leakage_guard.py` · `eval/decontam.py:26-39`)**: canary isolation (SWE-bench / BIG-bench aligned), hidden tests mounted read-only (physically unreachable during the agent run), trajectory-level cheating audits, proposer/test-set isolation (train/val/test split with stable hash assignment, never cherry-picked by score), and dual HF domain/query blocklists — competitors mostly ship a single "don't leak" prompt;
  3. **Statistical significance (`eval/significance.py`)**: exact McNemar with Edwards continuity correction, 1000× paired bootstrap 95% confidence intervals, Pass@k and difficulty tiers — when the score change is smaller than the interval it is noise, not a regression;
  4. **Four-mechanism plateau diagnosis (`eval/plateau.py`)**: separates insufficient statistical power / capability saturation / cross-model divergence / hard-subset bottleneck, explicitly discouraging pointless prompt micro-tweaks — no more "thirty prompt edits while the score stayed inside the noise band";
  5. **Failures get attributed (`eval/trajectory_analysis.py:30-54` + fault-side attribution)**: 9-class trajectory failure taxonomy with Pareto distribution plus immediate fault-side attribution (model hallucination / tool execution / flow / environment / evaluation / user instruction) — you learn WHERE the 30% failed;
  6. **Honest disclosure (environment disclosure grid + trajectory disclosure badges in `eval-lab/_ARCH.md:18`)**: model, thinking tier, judge model, harness version, case-set fingerprint, profile, tool policy, run budget, decontamination status, sampling and abort badges, plus budget-truncation `limit_hit` and decontamination `blocked` counts on screen — whether two scores are comparable is written down plainly, and "half the suite ran" never passes as "all green";
  7. **Multi-dimensional A/B and ablation**: layered evaluation (bare base → core → skills → memory), memory on/off A/B, three-arm skill comparison (none / baseline / candidate), and cross-profile matrix runs that automatically name the cases failing only under some configurations (`eval/matrix.py:119`).
  8. **Comprehensive Six-Competitor Analysis**:
     - **Hermes Agent**: evaluation is developer-only offline Python scripts (`evals/readtool/`, `evals/compaction/`) — terminal only, no GUI, no path from a real conversation to a private case;
     - **OpenClaw**: evaluation is decoupled from the main chat UI and relies on external public dataset files, leaving private business cases homeless; ClawEval is a model-selection CLI tool, not regression testing;
     - **Codex (ArkClaw)**: built around long-running execution and artifact archiving, with no built-in eval lab or regression suite — a model regression leaves manual re-investigation;
     - **DeepSeek-Harness**: focused on official model benchmarks with task standards tailored to specific reasoning models, and without multimodal Base64 cleanup, so image payloads can crash parsing;
     - **Pi / other general assistants**: no evaluation concept at all — one-way chat, and a pitfall you hit never becomes an asset;
     - **Additional reference · Anthropic `claude-api` skill**: its methodology (pairwise blind judging, frozen references, noise floors, three anti-overfitting guards, cost-climb search order) is genuinely advanced, but it targets developers who already have an eval set and can read code, and it only just launched — Myrm takes a different road (**GUI + private cases + productized reporting**) rather than copying the same shape;
  9. **SSOT Sources:** `temp-docs/materials/EVAL_LAB_BENCHMARK_ADVANTAGE.md` · `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/eval/` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/core/eval/` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-frontend/src/components/features/eval-lab/_ARCH.md`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-20 · Liberated PTC Control Flow, Strict Import Whitelisting & Runtime MCP Scope Gating · Eliminating Rigid AST Whitelist Rejections, In-Script RCE Bypass and Dead Scope Security Paths)

- **LiberatedPTCControlFlowAndRuntimeScopeGateSuite (Benchmarking Hermes / OpenClaw / Codex / DeepSeek-Harness / Pi / OpenCode against Programmatic Tool Calling flexibility, AST pre-execution verification, multi-alias path governance, and runtime MCP agent isolation):**
  1. **Liberated Turing-Complete Control Flow (`ptc_verifier.py`)**: Replaced rigid AST node whitelists with an import-level check permitting loops (`for`, `while`), branching (`if`), and error handling (`try-except`) while strictly confining imports to `skills.*`, `tools.*`, and safe stdlib modules (`json`, `re`, `math`, `datetime`, `collections`, `itertools`, `typing` etc.), supporting function alias imports (`from skills... import func as alias`).
  2. **Unified Multi-Alias Path Policy Enforcement (`batch_processor.py` & `ipc_proxy.py`)**: Synchronized path parameter detection across pre-execution static approval and runtime IPC proxy layers (`path`, `file_path`, `target`, `destination`, `filepath`), preventing traversal and workspace escape.
  3. **Runtime Agent-Scoped MCP Scope Gate (`ipc_proxy.py` & `scope_gate.py`)**: `ipc_proxy._check_scope_gate` enforces `EmployeeScopedMcpDiscoveryGate.verify_tool_invocation` on every PTC call. The policy key is the request's **`agent_id`** (carried separately from the chat `session_id` by `client_templates`/`prepare_mixin`), so a sandbox script cannot bypass the allowlist by changing the session id.
  4. **Unattended Read-Only Lease (`readonly_research_sandbox` · `research_lease.py` · `template_validation.py`)**: Cron jobs — and any run started by an untrusted external channel — execute under a per-run read-only lease with TTL expiry and graceful revoke (`agent_runner.py:898` grants, `:1018` releases; `readonly_lease_manager.py:60-73` lazily expires). Four independent layers enforce it, so no single missing check opens the path: (a) `script_all_spawns_readonly` AST-validates that every `spawn_subagent` call in a cron-bound workflow passes a literal `readonly=True`, rejecting the template at bind time and again at execution; (b) `coerce_spawn_readonly` forces children read-only when the parent holds no WRITE capability, even if the script requested otherwise; (c) write-class built-ins are blocklisted and MCP tools without a read-only annotation are stripped (`builder.py:114-128`); (d) subagent memory writes land in an ephemeral layer that bypasses the DB entirely (`ephemeral.py:326-332`). Competitors decide policy only when a call happens; none carries a session-level read-only state that is already in force before the first tool call.
  5. **Signed off in real browsers against a real model**: Three relevant Lane-C Chrome E2E sessions passed and sealed with MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview — MCP agent turn 180.26s, skill version/batch rollback 82.50s, and shell/eviction 135.90s — alongside 997 harness unit/coverage tests at 96.18% across the changed MCP/prepare modules.
  6. **Comprehensive Six-Competitor Analysis**:
     - **Hermes Agent**: Relies on turn-1 prompt suggestions and JSON tool calls without pre-execution AST syntax verification or Unix socket bearer token isolation; its `execute_code` guard approves the script as a whole and has no per-call or lease-level enforcement downstream;
     - **OpenClaw**: Defaults to bypass mode without AST-level path parameter extraction or employee-level MCP scope gate;
     - **Codex**: Confined to sandbox code execution without dynamic MCP skill binding or UDS IPC token proxy;
     - **DeepSeek-Harness**: Uses single `run_code` mode without fine-grained import whitelisting or agent-scoped discovery gates;
     - **Pi (oh-my-pi)**: Lacks AST pre-execution path policy enforcement and relies on manual tool exposure prefixes;
     - **OpenCode**: Lacks in-sandbox UDS token transport and agent-scoped runtime invocation gating.
  6. **SSOT Sources:** `temp-docs/materials/PROGRAMMATIC_TOOL_CALLING_ADVANTAGE.md` · `temp-docs/materials/SKILL_PERMISSION_SECURITY_ADVANTAGE.md` · `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/security/ptc_verifier.py` · `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/middlewares/approval/batch_processor.py` · `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/skills/mcp/ipc_proxy.py`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-19 · Grounded Completion Claims & Per-Turn Evidence Disclosure · Eliminating Confident "Tests Passed / Already Sent" Reports With Nothing Behind Them, and the Silent Loss of Unpersisted Deliverables)

- **ExecutionClaimGroundingAndEvidenceDisclosure (Benchmarking Hermes / OpenClaw / Codex / DeepSeek-Harness / Pi / OpenCode against trusting an agent's own completion claims):**
  1. **Load-bearing claims must match this run's real tool record (`myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/middlewares/completion/execution_claim_verifier.py`)**: three claim families are grounded — verification results ("all tests pass"), outbound/irreversible actions ("sent", "published", "deployed") and code changes ("fixed", "implemented"). Test claims accept only a real test runner that actually executed: collect-only runs, zero-collected output, `PATH=`-rewritten runners and read-only MCP calls never count as evidence; outbound claims need a sending MCP call, an outbound command or an outbound tool name — a successful read (history/list/get) or a plain file write does not. When a claim cannot be grounded, the turn is blocked with two acceptable fixes: do it now, or state exactly what was and was not done. Quoted documentation, conditional, negated, explanatory and follow-up sentences are excluded, so honest answers are never touched.
  2. **The user can see what a delivery is backed by (`agent/middlewares/completion/deliverable_confidence_tier.py` → server `stream_collector.py` → `deliverableTier` → WebUI badge)**: every answer carries an evidence tier — VERIFIED (real tests/lint/build or a passed review), ARTIFACT (files really written), RESEARCH (real sources), PLAN (nothing verifiable yet, shown **only** when the answer itself claims work, so small talk stays clean). Hovering the badge exposes the physical evidence (verification categories, written file paths, source counts).
  3. **Unpersisted deliverables are disclosed instead of lost**: when a code-writing turn never writes its code block to disk, the harness stages the draft under `.myrm/staged_artifacts/` and the answer ends with a notice listing the preserved drafts — so "the model pasted code and forgot to save it" no longer ends in a silent dead end.
  4. **Evidence survives approvals and concurrency**: a turn that is split across HITL approvals keeps one per-turn evidence ledger (idempotent, de-duplicated), and the guard's rejection budget is keyed by the turn's message id, so parallel chats, background reruns and cron turns can neither exhaust nor reset each other.
  5. **Signed off in a real browser against a real model**: four live Chrome cases (create a file → disclosed `ARTIFACT` tier + badge; create **and run** a script → its stdout appears in the answer; small talk → no badge at all; the disclosure surfaces fit a 355px message card) plus the seeded UI contract lane — every session `SUCCEEDED` with `cleanup.sealed=true`, alongside 359 harness unit/coverage tests, 277 static-contract tests and the 2,265-test code-execution regression.
  6. **Comprehensive Six-Competitor Analysis**:
     - **Hermes Agent**: records an Evidence Ledger of the commands the agent itself ran (passive bookkeeping); an agent can still announce completion without running anything, because nothing compares the final answer against the record before the user reads it;
     - **OpenClaw**: gateway- and cron-centric automation with plugin hooks; completion truthfulness is delegated to prompts and human review, and there is no per-answer, user-visible evidence tier;
     - **Codex**: tracks executed tool calls for approvals and audit, but the final report is free to claim whatever the model writes; there is no claim-to-trace grounding and no evidence badge in the UI;
     - **DeepSeek-Harness**: a benchmark-oriented harness whose notes cover PTY/ACL/adapter work; no mechanism aligns the assistant's finishing statement with the execution trace;
     - **Pi (oh-my-pi)**: heavy prompt- and sub-agent engineering; verification stays prompt-level, which a model can bypass — and the user cannot tell "this answer is backed by tests" from "this answer is a plan";
     - **OpenCode**: flexible plugin/skill ecosystem with a terminal-first UX; extensibility does not solve completion trust, and unpersisted deliverables are simply lost to the conversation;
  7. **SSOT Sources:** `temp-docs/materials/AGENT_VERIFICATION_ADVANTAGE.md` · `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/middlewares/completion/execution_claim_verifier.py` · `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/middlewares/completion/completion_guard_state.py` · `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/middlewares/completion/deliverable_confidence_tier.py` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/services/agent/streaming_support/stream_collector.py` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/tests/e2e/test_deliverable_disclosure_live_chrome_e2e.py`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-19 · Truthful @-Mention Context & Directory-Scoped Test Doubles · Eliminating Silent "File Too Large" Misreports, Order-Dependent Ghost Failures and Whole-Session Config Hijacks)

- **TruthfulUnreadableInputDisclosureAndScopedVerification (Benchmarking Hermes / OpenClaw / Codex / DeepSeek-Harness / Pi / OpenCode against honest unreadable-input reporting and self-verifying test infrastructure):**
  1. **Unreadable input is reported, never dressed up as success (`app/services/agent/params/mention.py`)**: a document whose parse yields no text — a scanned PDF, a blank deck — is now reported as `No extractable text (<size>), use file_read_tool to inspect if needed.` on **both** the workspace-file and stored-upload paths, replacing a misleading `Document too large (…)` that sent users chasing a size problem that did not exist; the inlining-limit branch reports the size of the content that could not be inlined instead of the file on disk, and the module carries **100% line coverage** (every refusal, truncation and parser-failure branch pinned by `tests/api/agent/test_mention_internal_paths.py`);
  2. **A test double can no longer hijack a production loader for the whole session (`tests/api/agent/conftest.py`)**: the agent-API user-config stub had been promoted to session scope by the root `pytest_plugins` registration, so unrelated suites silently read canned configs built from the developer's `.env.test`, and a mock left behind by fixture/monkeypatch teardown order poisoned later `patch()` originals — the classic "passes alone, fails in order" ghost. The stub is now guarded to its own directory, the three no-op overrides that existed only to defeat it are gone, and the suites that genuinely need configured configs (memory adapters, converter routing/retry/deep-research, factory dedup LLM) declare their own explicit stubs;
  3. **Security- and contract-sensitive endpoints are asserted through their SSOT**: control-plane internal endpoints set `settings.control_plane.telemetry_token` (not `os.environ`, which never reaches the settings singleton) and expect the guard's documented **401**; voice session payloads assert the OpenAI-realtime shape plus the always-declared reminder tools instead of a hard-coded count; the tool-error category vocabulary ships in every locale (`progressSteps.errorCategories.output_limit` across the locale SSOT), so a new failure category can never degrade the card to a raw enum value;
  4. **Signed off in a real browser against a real model**: a SHARED read-level Chrome E2E asserts the served shell payload carries the new error category **in the language the page actually runs in** (21.19s), and a PRIVATE live node attaches a mixed typed/scanned PDF through the real composer and requires the real model's answer to name the text-less pages (230.83s) — both sessions sealed and released, well inside the 10-minute per-test budget;
  5. **Comprehensive Six-Competitor Analysis**:
     - **Hermes Agent**: strong skill/self-evolution ecosystem, but its release cadence carries a visible session-state risk backlog and its verification leans on unit suites — neither a silent "file too large" misreport nor an escaped test double would surface before users hit them;
     - **OpenClaw**: gateway-centric automation with plugin hooks, but no first-party real-Chrome + real-model acceptance loop, so unreadable-attachment behaviour and cross-suite test leakage stay invisible until production;
     - **Codex**: closed, high-quality terminal sandbox; no GUI attachment pipeline to compare against and no browser-level acceptance evidence, so document-handling truthfulness is simply out of scope for its test suite;
     - **DeepSeek-Harness**: benchmark-oriented harness tuned for single-turn evaluation; no multi-turn attachment/mention pipeline whose failure modes could be asserted end-to-end;
     - **Pi (oh-my-pi)**: advisor-style dual-model design with a lighter verification surface — headless mocks cannot distinguish "parsed to nothing" from "too large", which is exactly the misreport Myrm now refuses to produce;
     - **OpenCode**: a well-engineered terminal-first coding agent with real but desktop-attached browser tooling and a file-centric config; its verification is unit-test centric, so a whole-session config hijack by a leaked test double would not be detected;
  6. **SSOT Sources:** `temp-docs/materials/ENTERPRISE_RELIABILITY_AND_TESTING_ADVANTAGE.md` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/services/agent/params/mention.py` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/tests/api/agent/conftest.py` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/tests/e2e/test_tool_error_category_locale_payload_chrome_e2e.py` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/tests/e2e/test_pdf_attachment_stream_chrome_e2e.py`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-19 · Chief-of-Staff Front Door & True-Sandbox Fleet Isolation · Eliminating Babysitting Multi-Chat Chaos, Unplanned Multi-Agent Clashes and Same-Host Security Illusions)

- **ChiefOfStaffFleetOrchestrationSuite (Benchmarking SpaceXAI / Grok Bot 5-Pillar Teams, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode against single-front-door routing, board-driven state machines, and hardware credential isolation):**
  1. **Chief-of-Staff Front Door vs Chat Babysitting**:
     - Replacing the fatigue of opening a new chat window for every task: users communicate with a single Chief of Staff that plans, delegates, and consolidates outcomes without writing business code itself, remaining silent during smooth task progression;
  2. **Board-Centric State Machine (Work Lives on a Board, Not in a Chat)**:
     - Eliminating multi-agent collisions where bots without shared state clash or overwrite files: tasks advance across a 31-component Kanban pipeline (`Todo → Working → Blocked → Ready for Review → Done`), exchanging dependencies via cards and resolving blocks autonomously;
  3. **True-Sandbox Fleet Isolation vs Same-Computer Security Illusion**:
     - Eliminating the vulnerability acknowledged by Grok Bot (where all bots share one cloud computer and access each other's logins/credentials): Myrm enforces physical `Agent-in-Sandbox` isolation where each specialist agent runs in a dedicated ephemeral sandbox with its own scoped memory and hardware vault credentials;
  4. **5 a.m. Autonomous Standup & Unattended Draft Staging**:
     - At dawn, agents convene an autonomous standup to realign dependencies and review nightly code audits. Tasks encountering human approvals during overnight runs gracefully stage as pending drafts instead of expiring after 10-minute timeouts;
  5. **Dual-Role Memory Leak Detection & Automated Agent Fission**:
     - Proactively detects when a single agent's memory spans conflicting domains (e.g. code review vs marketing copy) and offers a one-click recommendation to fisson into specialized domain agents with clean isolated personas;
  6. **Direct Competitor Comparison Matrix**:
     - **Grok Bot (SpaceXAI Architecture)**: Groundbreaking 5-pillar operational philosophy, but acknowledges that all bots share the same cloud machine without credential boundaries; lacks native physical sandbox isolation;
     - **OpenClaw / Codex / Pi**: Operate primarily as single-agent or terminal-centric sessions without autonomous standup routines or board-driven multi-agent state machines.

- **SubscriptionBridgeSecuritySuite (Benchmarking Hermes Antigravity OAuth, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode against personal subscription reuse, account-ban defense, and quota failover):**
  1. **Ban-Defense Pacing & Jitter Smoothing**:
     - Third-party agent scripts often trigger anti-bot bans by sending bursts of high-concurrency requests through user subscription tokens. Myrm injects humanized pacing and random millisecond jitter, smoothing traffic patterns to match native IDE behavior and isolating CLI subprocesses in unprivileged ephemeral sandboxes;

     - Resolving the sudden task abortion when a single account's 7-day rolling window is depleted: Myrm pools 2–3 subscription accounts with weighted load balancing.   3. **Headless PTY OAuth Relay**:
     - Solving the silent hang on remote Linux/SSH servers and cloud control-plane sandboxes where official CLIs wait indefinitely for standard input: Myrm relays browser-approved auth codes via a non-blocking pseudo-terminal (PTY) file pipe, enabling zero-friction remote cloud logins;
  4. **Tool Adaptation Gateway & SOUL Fidelity**:
     - Model calls to proprietary CLI tools (e.g. `run_command`, `view_file`) are transparently remapped into Myrm's internal secure toolsets, subject to four-tier physical approval gates and sandbox confinement;

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-19 · Careful Execution Discipline & Three-Fixes Circuit Breakers · Eliminating Runaway Guessing Loops, Parallel Workspace File Collisions and Memory Scope Creep)

- **CarefulExecutionDisciplineSuite (Benchmarking Oh-My-Hermes 3.0.2, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode against automated debugging runaway loops, multi-agent file overwrite collisions, and memory command poisoning):**
  1. **Three-Fixes Failure Circuit Breaker**:
     - When an agent encounters the same compilation or test failure, traditional agents repeatedly attempt 8–10 blind edits, mutating irrelevant files and burning thousands of tokens. Myrm enforces a hard circuit breaker: 3 consecutive failed fixes triggers an instant atomic snapshot rollback, pausing execution and presenting an architectural assumption critique to the user;
  2. **Tool-Level Physical File FenceGuard**:
     - Beyond fragile verbal prompt rules ("do not modify module B"), Myrm mounts physical write barriers directly on file tools (`write_file`, `edit_file`, `shell`). Touching protected paths throws immediate `PermissionDenied` errors, eliminating write collisions across concurrent subagents;
  3. **Autonomous In-Scope Decisions & Capped Review Rounds**:
     - Replaces both reckless unilateral mutations and paralyzing confirmation dialog floods. In-scope minor decisions are taken autonomously and logged to a tamper-proof decision ledger, while review rounds are strictly bounded to prevent endless back-and-forth prompts;
  4. **SSOT Service Contract Gate & Canonical Memory Neutralizer**:
     - Enforces a single authoritative contract (OpenAPI / Schema / Proto) per service boundary, failing builds instantly on contract drift; purges imperative "you must..." commands from memory and anchors single-use approvals to prevent cross-model semantic drift when switching models;
  5. **Direct Competitor Comparison Matrix**:
     - **Oh-My-Hermes (v3.0.2)**: Introduced the three-fix rule and SkillEx guidelines, but relies primarily on terminal prompts and local sandboxes; lacks cloud control-plane multi-sandbox orchestration;
     - **OpenClaw**: Multi-agent file coordination depends on prompt conventions, leaving files vulnerable to write collisions under long contexts;
     - **Codex / DeepSeek-Harness / Pi / OpenCode**: No circuit-breaker mechanisms for repeated debugging failures; no imperative memory sanitization.

Certified with a green `VERIFIED_BACKUP` shield only after 100% pass;
  3. **Blue-Green Zero-Downtime Upgrades with Shadow Regression Gate**:

Any regression triggers instant 0-cost rollback to Blue;

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-19 · Physical Concept Wireframes & Zero-NPM Interactive Prototype Engine · Eliminating Flat Abstract Box-and-Arrow Diagrams and Heavy Unsandboxed WebGL Mobile Freezes)

- **PhysicalConceptWireframeSuite (Benchmarking Hermes Agent + Anatomy, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode against system concept prototyping — explaining complex distributed locks, memory caching, and multi-service queues through interactive machines vs abstract flowcharts):**
  1. **The Object is the Explanation (Physical Metaphor)**:
     - Myrm incorporates isometric mechanical concept modeling: caching is visualized as pin-tumbler locks, GPU dispatchers as gantry plotters, and queues as conveyor mechanisms. Users grasp architecture instantly without decoding abstract UML boxes;
  2. **Zero NPM Dependencies & Pure Geometry Computation**:
     - Built purely on Node standard math primitives (lathe profiles, tube segments, isometric raycasters); requires zero `npm install` inside user execution sandboxes, avoiding supply-chain risks and network timeouts;

     - Automated build-time audit verifies zero clipping across 3D part assemblies (`audit passed`) and zero disconnected line ends (`line ends: 0`), enforcing professional manufacturing-grade diagram precision;
  4. **GPU Energy Guard & Mobile Graceful Degradation**:
     - Automatically caps DPR $\le$ 1.5, freezes `requestAnimationFrame` when the artifact is scrolled out of viewport, and adapts to lightweight static SVG on mobile Telegram/H5 sessions, preventing laptop battery drain and mobile browser crashes;
  5. **Prototype-to-Component Bidirectional Bridge**:

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-18 · Search Readiness Verdict Before the Turn & Bounded Probe Wall Clock · Eliminating the Silent Hour-Long Search Config Trap Where a Half-Dead Self-Hosted Provider Stalls the First Message)

- **SearchReadinessPreflightSuite (Benchmarking Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode against a search provider that is configured but unusable — a mistyped key, an expired credential, or a self-hosted SearXNG that accepts the connection and never answers):** competitors all call search at use time and only report failure after the user has already asked something. Myrm decides before the turn: every configured credential is probed for real, a provider chain is probed as one unit so a dead first hop does not reject every message while a backup still answers, and the verdict is cached for 60 seconds and shared by every entry point (Web, IM channels, voice, cron, kanban boards, goals, eval, Wiki).
- **Bounded probe (measured):** the probe carries its own wall clock instead of inheriting the provider client's retry budget. A provider that accepts the TCP connection but never replies used to hold the readiness gate for **60.5s**; it now gives up at **25.0s** — same verdict (`unavailable`), less than half the wait. Connection-refused and invalid-key providers already cost 1.6–1.7s and are unaffected. No credential value is exempt: the probe always makes a real request, so a verdict of "available" always corresponds to a response that actually happened.
- **Honest boundary:** this is a fast liveness check, not an SLA guarantee. The 25s bound is a conservative value derived from the client's own single-attempt hang detection, not calibrated against a distribution of real provider latencies.
- **SSOT Sources:** `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/services/integrations/search_verify.py` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/tests/services/integrations/test_search_verify.py` · `temp-docs/materials/SEARCH_INTELLIGENCE_ADVANTAGE.md`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-18 · Per-Page PDF Coverage Disclosure & Scanned-Page Delivery · Eliminating the Mixed-Document Trap Where a Typed-plus-Scanned PDF Is Served as "Fully Read" While Its Scanned Pages Silently Vanish)

- **PdfCoverageDisclosureSuite (Benchmarking Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode against the mixed-document case — a contract whose body is typeset text but whose signature pages are scans, and the same again behind an auxiliary vision model or with no vision at all):** Myrm reports what it actually delivered instead of implying full coverage:
  1. **Per-Page Emptiness, Not a Whole-Document Character Count**:
     - Text-vs-scanned is decided per page by whether that page's body carries any text at all. The previous whole-document threshold classified a 3-typed-page + 5-scan-page contract as "has text" and then served only the typed pages, while `parsedPages` still equalled `pageCount`, so nothing anywhere said pages 4-8 were missing;
  2. **Three Distinct Shortfall Channels, Never Interchangeable**:
     - `image_trace.truncated` = the embedded-figure ceiling cut the scan short; `parsedPages < pageCount` = the delivery window cut the document short; `pagesWithoutText` = a page inside the delivered window has no text layer. Collapsing them into one flag would double-report one gap and hide the other two;
  3. **"Sent as Images" Is Now Verifiable, Not Assumed**:
     - Every delivered image carries its real source page (`PDFImageContent.page_number`, surfaced as `PDFImageContent.pageNumber` on the wire). The composer intersects that set with `pagesWithoutText` and emits **two** notices — the pages that really travelled as images, and the pages the per-delivery ceiling withheld. Claiming "sent as images" for pages that never left the server was the first version of this copy, and it was false;
  4. **Scanned Pages Reach the Model Through the Same Three Routes as Plain Images**:
     - Native vision receives page images; a text-only model with an auxiliary chain configured receives each page described as text, labelled with its real page number; with neither, the OCR tier handles plain images that previously got only a metadata line. Page numbers are source pages, never positions within the delivered batch — mislabelling pages 4-8 as 1-5 sends the model to the wrong pages;
  5. **Direct Competitor Comparison Matrix**:
     - **Hermes**: Emits a `[NEEDS OCR: pages N … MISSING]` prefix and counts per-page density (`tools/read_extract.py:178-186`, `PDF_EMPTY_PAGE_CHARS = 20`) — the closest competitor, and the design we credit for the prefix placement; it does **not** distinguish pages that were sent from pages the delivery ceiling withheld, and bulk OCR needs marker-pdf (~3-5GB PyTorch plus ~2.5GB models);
     - **OpenClaw**: Resolves PDFs through native provider parsing (`src/agents/tools/pdf-native-providers.ts`); no per-page coverage report reaches the user;
     - **Codex**: Native PDF input is still an open request (`openai/codex#1797`); scanned pages require rendering to PNG and passing `--image` by hand, and there is no coverage concept;
     - **DeepSeek-Harness**: No PDF extraction path in the tree at all;
     - **Pi (pi-mono)**: No PDF pipeline;
     - **OpenCode**: No PDF pipeline;
  6. **Where Myrm Is Genuinely Ahead — And Where It Is Not**: We are **not** claiming competitors read scanned PDFs poorly — Hermes reads them, and Codex's vision path works once the pages are rendered. We are ahead in **honesty about coverage**: we are the only one of the six that separates "delivered as an image" from "withheld by the delivery ceiling", and the only one that reports a per-page gap to the user *and* to the model through the same field. We are behind on one axis worth naming: there is no page-range read parameter (`pages="4-8"`) as in Codex's Read tool; our answer is the `parse_mode='structure'` outline plus the `[Partial Read]` hint, which suits a GUI user better than remembering page numbers but is not the same ergonomics;
  7. **100% Full-Stack Testing Verification**: Real unmocked Chrome WebUI E2E — `test_pdf_attachment_stream_chrome_e2e.py::test_pdf_attachment_discloses_scanned_pages_live_chrome_e2e` **1 passed in 213.19s** with `outcome: PASSED` and `cleanup.sealed`; the PDF went in through the real `<input type="file">` + `DataTransfer`, the real frontend called `/files/extract-pdf`, and real `minimax/MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview` inference answered **"共 5 页，其中第 4、5 页没有可提取的文本层（为扫描图片页）"** against a 3-typed + 2-scan fixture. Plus edge-fixture matrix (pure scan → `[1,2,3]`; pure text → `[]`; 1+1 → `[2]`), 1235 harness cases, 17 server API cases, 269 frontend cases, 6-locale parity, 0 lint errors, 0 type errors.
- **SSOT Sources:** `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/toolkits/file_parsers/pdf/pdf.py` (`pages_with_text` / `pages_without_text`) · `.../pdf/pdf_content_extractor.py` (`PDFImageContent.page_number`, `pages_without_text`) · `.../agent/meta_tools/file_ops/utils/pdf_reader.py` (three-route delivery, `[PDF coverage]` prefix, `[Partial Read]`, RAG caveat) · `.../file_ops/core/file_read_handlers.py` · `.../toolkits/file_parsers/ocr.py` (RapidOCR) · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/api/files/pdf_extract.py` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-frontend/src/store/chat/multimodalBuilder.ts` · `myrm-agent-server/tests/e2e/test_pdf_attachment_stream_chrome_e2e.py` · `temp-docs/materials/FILE_SYSTEM_INTELLIGENCE_ADVANTAGE.md`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-18 · Per-Session UI State Isolation & Single-Source Clarify Routing · Eliminating Cross-Chat Plan Wipes, Foreign Milestone Numbers & Approval Cards Resuming the Wrong Turn)

- **PerSessionUiStateIsolationSuite (Benchmarking Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode against the multi-chat case where a background conversation's terminal events overwrite the single-slot panels of the conversation the user is reading, and where a clarification card's resume route is inferred from whichever chat happens to be open):** Myrm keeps every visible panel bound to the session that owns it:
  1. **Ownership-Gated Writes for Single-Slot Panels**:
     - The plan panel, context-compaction milestone, subagent/fission strip, and approval surfaces all write only when the stream belongs to the open chat (`isStreamForOpenChat`), evaluated **after** the lazy import so a chat switch during load drops the write instead of landing it in the next chat;
  2. **Event-Carried Attribution Instead of Ambient State**:
     - An approval card carries the streaming turn's own `chatId` and `actionMode` (captured at send time), and a clarification's resume-vs-legacy route is decided **only** from the event's own `source` — the exact rule the server persists — never from the mode of the chat on screen;
  3. **No Dead-End Blocking Prompts**:
     - Blocking consent and takeover surfaces deliberately stay answerable for background turns too; unanswered interrupts are reopened server-side and pending approvals are re-fetched globally, so parallel sessions never leave a turn waiting forever. This is the honest trade-off: those panels are single-slot, so simultaneous requests are shown one after another rather than side by side, but none is lost;
  4. **Direct Competitor Comparison Matrix**:
     - **Hermes**: Buckets subagent progress per session (`apps/desktop/src/store/subagents.ts:54`), so switching sessions is naturally isolated — the principle we match, but it covers subagents only;
     - **OpenClaw**: Keys pending approvals by id in a registry (`src/shared/*approval-registry*`) and never reads an ambient chat mode;
     - **Codex**: Confirmation lives in the editor's single run; there is no multi-chat panel state to misattribute, and correspondingly no cross-session panel isolation to offer;
     - **DeepSeek-Harness**: Attributes approval UI by `approval.callId` (`packages/client/ui-approval/src/client/ApprovalPanel.tsx`), but ships no plan/milestone panels, so the class of defect is out of scope for it;
     - **Pi (pi-mono)**: Optimised for a single lightweight session; no multi-pane panel-ownership model;
     - **OpenCode**: Scoped to the current code diff; no cross-task panel state;
  5. **Where Myrm Is Genuinely Ahead — And Where It Is Not**: We are **not** claiming competitors lack session isolation (Hermes, OpenClaw and DeepSeek-Harness all do it). We are ahead in **breadth and single-sourcing**: one shared ownership gate covers plan, approval, clarification, and compaction panels, and the clarify resume rule exists as **one definition** shared by the live handlers and the DB-hydrate path, so backend and frontend cannot disagree about which route a card takes;
  6. **100% Full-Stack Testing Verification**: Real unmocked Chrome WebUI E2E — `test_clarify_skip_chrome_e2e.py::test_clarify_skip_button_resumes_agent_in_real_chat` **1 passed in 372.02s**, `test_goal_focus_chrome_e2e.py` **1 passed in 171.80s**, `test_reasoning_anchor_and_compaction_chrome_e2e.py` **1 passed in 26.29s**, `test_browser_takeover_chrome_e2e.py::test_extension_takeover_banner_shows_actions_and_dismisses_on_done` **1 passed in 27.25s** — all four with `outcome: PASSED` and `cleanup.sealed`; real `minimax/MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview` inference throughout, asserted down to the persisted user message (`chat_user_message_count >= 1`); 18 focused unit cases green; 0 lint errors, 0 type errors.
- **SSOT Sources:** `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-frontend/src/store/chat/messageStream/handlers/handlerDeps.ts` · `.../handlers/completionEvents.ts` · `.../handlers/agentControlEvents.ts` · `.../handlers/toolsProgressEvents.ts` · `.../handlers/statusStreamProgressSteps.ts` · `.../messageStream/streamHelpers.ts` · `myrm-agent-server/tests/e2e/test_clarify_skip_chrome_e2e.py` · `temp-docs/materials/HITL_APPROVAL_CONCURRENCY_ADVANTAGE.md`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-18 · Cross-Host Reduced-Motion Contract · Eliminating the Main-Window-Only Guard That Leaves Sandboxed Widgets, Extension Panels and the Desktop Lock Screen Animating)

- **CrossHostReducedMotionSuite (Benchmarking Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode against the case where a person with the OS "reduce motion" setting turned on still gets functional animation — spinning loaders in agent-generated widgets, pulsing indicators in the browser extension, and a full-screen desktop curtain that keeps breathing):** Motion is requested at the document level, so a rule written once in the app stylesheet stops at the edge of that document. Myrm treats the preference as a contract that every rendering host must satisfy:
  1. **Five Independent Documents, Each Guarded**:
     - Main WebUI (`src/app/globals.css`), the sandboxed artifact/MCP-app `<iframe srcdoc>` whose `<style>` is built by `src/lib/widget-theme-bridge.ts`, the extension sidepanel (`sidepanel.css`, 14 motion rules) and popup (`popup.html`, 2 rules), and the Tauri privacy curtain rendered from `privacy_curtain_page.rs` (2 rules) each carry their own `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` guard;
  2. **The Animation Engine CSS Cannot Reach**:
     - `framer-motion` animates through the Web Animations API and defaults to `reducedMotion: "never"`, so no media query can stop it. The page root is wrapped in `<MotionConfig reducedMotion="user">`: positional transforms collapse to instant, while opacity fades are kept so state changes remain legible;
  3. **The Unmount Contract — `0.01ms`, Never `animation: none`**:
     - Radix overlays unmount by matching `animationend`. Writing the guard as `animation: none` silences that event and produces a dialog that cannot be closed — an accessibility fix that locks the UI. Myrm caps `animation-duration` at `0.01ms` with `animation-iteration-count: 1` so the event still fires;
  4. **Direct Competitor Comparison Matrix (measured from committed source, not assumed)**:
     - **OpenClaw**: ships the *same* universal guard in `ui/src/styles/base.css` — `*, *::before, *::after` with `animation-duration: 0.01ms`, `animation-iteration-count: 1`, `scroll-behavior: auto`. We are level with it here, not ahead;
     - **DeepSeek-Harness**: also ships a global `0.01ms` cap (45 references across 25 files). Level with us on the main window;
     - **OpenCode / Pi**: both handle the preference, but per-component rather than through one universal block (44 references / 38 files and 39 / 26 respectively); OpenCode additionally hand-checks `matchMedia` inside individual components (`packages/ui/src/navigation/segmented-control/segmented-control.tsx:111`) instead of applying a global motion policy to the framer-motion tree it ships;
     - **Hermes**: no guard at all. Its desktop renderer carries 9 motion rules (`apps/hermes-buddy-desktop/src/renderer/styles.css`) and its prediction panel 3 more (`src/predict/predict-panel.css`), none conditioned on the preference — the only real motion gap found among the six;
     - **Codex**: no `prefers-reduced-motion` anywhere (0 hits), but its inline-visualization iframe stylesheet (`codex-rs/tui/src/assets/inline_visualization/visualize.css`) declares no animation at all, so there is no gap to claim against it — an honest zero rather than a defect;
  5. **Where Myrm Is Genuinely Ahead — And Where It Is Not**: We are **not** claiming competitors lack reduced-motion support. On "one media query in the main window", OpenClaw and DeepSeek-Harness are our equals, and presenting that as a differentiator would be false advertising. The measurable lead is narrower and harder: **coverage breadth** (five separate documents versus a main-window-only guard), **engine coverage** (WAAPI-driven motion that CSS cannot stop), and **a regression gate**;
  6. **Build-Time Gate Against Silent Regressions**:
     - One correct stylesheet does not survive the next animation someone adds. `verify:reduced-motion` is registered in `run-lint-gates.ts` and scans **9,244 files**, requiring every standalone document host to carry its own guard, with 2 explicit allowlist entries and reasons. No competitor was found to have an equivalent gate;
  7. **100% Real-Browser Verification (no computed-style guessing)**:
     - Four assertions on the live dev server: baseline `2s / infinite`; under reduce `1e-05s / 1`; the preference flipped **both ways without a reload** returns to `2s / infinite` and collapses again; and `animationend` still fires while reduced — the unmount contract above. All four green, **1 passed in 20.97s**;
  8. **Real-Model Full-Chain Verification**: A prompt sent through the chat bridge with the preference emulated produced a real streamed answer from `minimax/MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview`, asserted from the rendered turn snapshot — **1 passed in 115.73s**;
  9. **Known Boundary, Stated Rather Than Hidden**: Some prebuilt showcase templates draw with Canvas `requestAnimationFrame`, which a CSS media query cannot influence; Myrm does not yet degrade those at runtime and makes no promotional claim about them. `prefers-reduced-transparency` and `forced-colors` are likewise out of scope for this work.

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-18 · Oversized SVG & Complex Graphics Preview · Eliminating Multi-Second Freezes and Progressive Degradation)

- **SvgPreviewThresholdRasterizationSuite (Benchmarking Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode across oversized SVG / complex graphic previews where competitors synchronously sanitize and inline tens of thousands of DOM nodes, blocking the main thread for 0.8-7 seconds, degrading further to freeze-level when several large files are opened in a row, and where sibling products have shipped XSS-prone inline SVG injection):** Myrm delivers a size-split SVG preview engine:
  1. **Threshold Split — Oversized Files Switch to Native Browser Rasterization (Open-source Frontend Product Layer)**:
     - Regular-size content keeps the sanitized inline render (interactive, theme-adaptive);
     - Content at or above the 1MB threshold switches to the browser's native image rasterization path, skipping synchronous sanitization and mass DOM construction; object URLs are revoked on content change and unmount, and load failures degrade to an explicit message;
  2. **Safety Aligned with Industry Best Practice**:
     - Oversized files load through an image context where the browser blocks SVG scripts and external resources by spec — the same direction industry leaders moved after their security incidents, adopted here as the default path;
  3. **Direct Competitor Comparison Matrix**:
     - **Hermes**: previews artifacts with unsanitized-size inline rendering, blocking the main thread on large files just the same;
     - **Codex**: rasterizes in a background sidecar with a 256KiB source cap in the TUI; the desktop app has shipped freeze issues from inlining large content (issues #34863 / #21232) — same rasterization direction, implemented here with native browser capability while keeping interactive inline rendering below 1MB;
     - **Pi**: moved from inline SVG injection to image loading after an XSS report (issue #3552) — same safety direction, while we also split by size for performance;
     - **OpenClaw**: chunk-renders large text; no dedicated SVG performance path;
     - **DeepSeek-Harness / OpenCode**: no web artifact preview surface; not comparable;
  4. **Real Full-Stack Verification (Zero Mocks)**: unit 7/7 (threshold split / boundary / object URL revocation / failure degradation / script stripping); real-Chrome injection E2E proving both render paths and zero script execution; real-chain E2E (six workspace files, small SVG inline plus honest degradation past the read ceiling); real-model (MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview) end-to-end: the model writes an SVG file and the workspace browser renders the preview (passed in 131.75s); a 3.4MB graphic opens in 0.25-0.3s, down from 0.8-7.2s (Chrome measured, 2.8-24x faster).
- **SSOT Sources:** `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-frontend/src/components/features/artifacts/renderers/MediaPreview.tsx` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/tests/e2e/test_workspace_rich_media_preview_chrome_e2e.py` · `.../test_agent_svg_artifact_preview_chrome_e2e.py` · `temp-docs/materials/UI_STREAMING_RENDER_RELIABILITY_ADVANTAGE.md`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-20 · Dual-Track MCP Routing, Runtime Agent Scope Gate & Skill Version SSOT · Honest Cache Boundary)

- **DualTrackMCPAndScopeGateSuite (Hermes / OpenClaw / Codex / DeepSeek-Harness / Pi / OpenCode):** Myrm keeps the MCP surface small and accountable instead of claiming a cache that never warmed routing:
  1. **Direct FC + on-demand PTC**: lightweight servers keep full parameter-schema and description fidelity on Direct FC; heavyweight servers demote to PTC skills loaded only when needed. The schema-token router and warm connection pool prevent full-schema context blowups.
  2. **No fake schema cache**: the old cross-session schema disk cache was removed. It wrote every route and only logged a cache hit on failure; it never changed routing or cold start. Myrm does **not** claim a 0.18 ms / 10,000× cold-start win.
  3. **Agent-scoped MCP runtime gate**: `mcp_ids` + `mcp_tool_selections` define the per-agent server/tool whitelist; `ipc_proxy._check_scope_gate` enforces it on every PTC call using the request's `agent_id` (not the chat session id). A sandbox script cannot bypass the allowlist by calling a tool the agent was not granted.
  4. **Skill version SSOT**: skill version snapshots and batch rollback live in the server DB (`write_orchestrator` + `skill_optimization` + `mutation_batch_service`); the harness no longer keeps a second JSONL history.
  5. **Verification**: 997 harness unit/coverage tests pass (96.18% across the changed MCP/prepare modules); three real Chrome E2E sessions passed and sealed — MCP agent turn 180.26s, skill rollback 82.50s, shell/eviction 135.90s — with MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview.
  6. **Honest competitor matrix**: Hermes has a product-layer skill ledger/usage lifecycle; OpenClaw has skill versions plus per-server MCP policies; Codex has MCP tool exposure; DeepSeek-Harness has a skill catalog and MCP client; Pi has four-state MCP exposure; OpenCode has permission gates and skills. Myrm's differentiation is the **combination** of runtime agent-scoped MCP enforcement and server-side skill rollback SSOT — not a claim that competitors lack every individual feature.

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-18 · Single-Authority History & Frozen Skill-Catalog Baseline · Eliminating the Per-Turn History Re-Feed That Shatters the Prompt Prefix Every Turn)

- **SkillCatalogMidSessionDriftTailDeltaNoteSuite (Benchmarking Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode against the two quietest long-session cache killers — re-feeding the whole conversation to the model every turn, and rewriting the first message whenever the skill catalog changes):** Myrm makes the model's own thread the single authority for the conversation and freezes the catalog baseline:
  1. **One Authority Per Turn (Closed-source Harness Framework Core)**:
     - Each turn declares its relationship to the thread — `continue` (the LangGraph thread *is* the history, so only the new message is sent; the prefix stays byte-identical), `replace` (the thread is cleared and the conversation is rebuilt from the stored record), or `append` (the untouched default for IM/cron/wakeup callers). A `continue` resolves back to `replace` when the thread is empty, and a `replace` that cannot clear the thread fails the turn rather than silently doubling the history;
     - The product half proves the thread still mirrors the stored chat with a **digest token** (`chats.history_sync_token` = sha256 of the compacted summary plus the ordered ids of the active branch after the compaction anchor). A match at turn start means `continue`; anything else — a failed read, a missing record, undo, edit-resend, regenerate, sibling switch, compaction, or a server-appended notice — answers `replace` and the next turn rebuilds. The token is written **only after a turn completed successfully, uncancelled, non-fatal and not waiting on the user**;
  2. **A Frozen Baseline Delivered as a Tail Note**:
     - The bound-skill catalog (`<bound_skills hash hidden_count>`) is written into the first user message once and **never rewritten in place**. When the catalog drifts mid-session, the turn prepends a `<bound_skills_update hash>` note in front of the new message — **behind the cached prefix** — carrying the **full current list** (it supersedes every earlier list, and states `No skills available.` when the set empties). The note is sent only when the difference actually changes, reads the last-delivered catalog back out of the thread's own messages (so it survives a reconnect or a fresh per-turn agent), and after compaction erases the block the baseline is re-delivered automatically;
  3. **Direct Competitor Comparison Matrix**:
     - **Hermes**: ships the same tail-note idea for skill drift (`agent/skills_index_delta.py`, ported from `cloudflare/cloudflare-os#267` by both projects) — but its note is a *diff accumulator* that must be stacked to reconstruct the truth, while Myrm's note is a self-describing *full list* where the newest note alone is complete;
     - **OpenClaw / Codex / Pi / DeepSeek-Harness / OpenCode**: no equivalent "thread mirrors the stored conversation" authority and no frozen-baseline-plus-tail-delta chain — the common pattern is to rebuild context or re-feed history each turn, so the prefix is unstable and a mid-session skill change stays invisible until compaction;
  4. **Verified End-to-End (real code, no mocks)**: harness `turn_history` at **100%** and `session_continuity` at **100%** line coverage (`skill_catalog_delivery` 99%, `skill_catalog_turn` 98%); server `history_sync`, `turn_history_sync` and `chat_history_sync_repo` at **100%**; **83 harness tests + 87 server tests pass**, and a real-UI Chrome E2E over two same-chat turns plus a rewind passed **twice consecutively** against a live MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview.
- **SSOT Sources:** `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/agent/_internals/turn_history.py` · `.../agent/skills/runtime/skill_catalog_delivery.py` · `.../agent/_internals/skill_catalog_turn.py` · `.../runtime/context/session/session_continuity.py` · `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/services/chat/history_sync.py` · `.../app/services/agent/stream_session/turn_history_sync.py` · `temp-docs/materials/PROMPT_CACHE_ADVANTAGE.md`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-18 · Retrieval Fail-Open Stop-Transparency Fix · Eliminating Press-Once-Keeps-Running Stops, Broken Tool Timeouts & Leaked Store Handles)

- **StopStaysStoppedRetrievalCancellationSuite (Benchmarking Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode across long-running tool calls, where a user pressing Stop can be silently converted into a normal "degraded success" result — the turn keeps running, the tool-level timeout loses its ceiling, and the cancelled work keeps holding its database/vector handles):** Myrm closes the loop between the UI Stop button and the framework's one cancellation-classification rule:
  1. **Stop Is Never Absorbed by Fail-Open Degradation (Closed-source Harness Framework Core)**:
     - Memory retrieval must fail open (a hanging store degrades recall instead of blocking the turn). Previously its embed and claim-graph stages used a bare `except BaseException`, which also caught `CancelledError` — in Python 3.13 `CancelledError` is **not** an `Exception` (measured `issubclass(CancelledError, Exception) == False`), so it was being converted into a plain degraded result **before** it could ever reach the framework's existing boundary (`utils.runtime.cancellation.reraise_if_cancelled`, a single SSOT that uses `Task.cancelling()` to tell a genuinely aimed stop apart from an SDK-leaked cancellation);
     - Both handlers now hand the cancellation to that rule. A stop aimed at the task propagates immediately; SDK-leaked cancellations and already-cancelled child tasks returned by `gather(return_exceptions=True)` still degrade exactly as before;
  2. **A Stopped Collect Leaves No Store Task Behind**:
     - `asyncio.wait` does not cancel the tasks it awaits, so a stop during collection left store searches holding their SQLite/vector handles. The collect stage now cancels its own store tasks and awaits them before letting the cancellation continue;
  3. **Direct Competitor Comparison Matrix**:
     - **Hermes**: Python, but exposes no cancellation-classification rule at the retrieval layer; a stop inside a slow tool has no single authority to consult;
     - **OpenClaw / DeepSeek-Harness / Codex**: TypeScript with `AbortController`/`AbortSignal`, so the "broad catch swallows the abort" class of defect does not exist for them — they are structurally immune rather than more correct by design;
     - **Pi / OpenCode**: no equivalent single classification boundary; a broad `except` around a tool call can still turn a stop into a normal result;
  4. **Verified End-to-End (real code, no mocks)**: with a real `MemoryManager` and the real tool middleware, a real `task.cancel()` moved from `stop ABSORBED -> returned []` to `stop PROPAGATED`, the turn from `CONTINUES WITH: degraded(error)` to `STOP REACHED THE TURN -> cancelled` in **0.50s**, and leaked store tasks from **1 to 0**; 4 mutations each fail exactly their own regression test; 92% line coverage on the changed module.
- **SSOT Sources:** `myrm-agent-harness/src/myrm_agent_harness/toolkits/memory/_internal/search_service.py` · `.../utils/runtime/cancellation.py` · `.../agent/middlewares/tooling/_tool_execution_lifecycle.py` · `temp-docs/materials/ERROR_RECOVERY_ROBUSTNESS_ADVANTAGE.md`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-15 · Items 77-79 Multi-Gateway Fleet Router, Graph Applicability Triage Gate & Dual Orthogonal Reviewer Gate · Eliminating LLM Self-Rationalization Bias, Graph Token Explosion & Host Lock-in)

     - **Open-source Server + Frontend Product Tier**: REST API `/fleet/gateways` connects to `FleetWideProfileRailCard.tsx` for distributed fleet topology visibility;
  4. **Direct Competitor Comparison Matrix**:
     - **Hermes**: Single-process CLI; review relies on the same conversational context model with self-justification bias; lacks topology triage and drops commands when offline;
     - **OpenClaw**: Fixed rigid pipelines without graph applicability triage; lacks clean-context blind subagents;
     - **Codex (CLI)**: Closed-source blackbox; review limited to static PR diffs without structured auto-fix dispatch;
     - **DeepSeek-Harness**: Statically launches complex graphs; lacks dynamic throttling and graceful early reduction, risking total job failure on 429 quota exhaustion;
     - **Pi**: Community plugin assembly; lacks multi-host gateway topology routing and offline buffer recovery;
     - **OpenCode**: Single read-only subagent; lacks orthogonal review gates and cannot intercept subtle type-safety or edge-case regressions;
  5. **100% Full-Stack Testing Verification**: Harness tests 19/19 passed, Server tests 15/15 passed, Frontend Vitest 15/15 passed in <1s, `bunx oxlint` 0 warnings 0 errors, all components strict ≤400 lines, 0 `Any` types, accessible live output 100% compliant.

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-02 · Cloud Sandbox Sub-Second Hibernation, Dedicated Persistent Volume & Headless Virtual Display Computer Use Suite · Eliminating Mac Mini 7x24 Electricity Wear, VPS Screenless Blindness & Cold Start State Loss)

- **CloudSandboxSubSecondHibernationAndHeadlessComputerUseSuite (Benchmarking DigitalOcean Managed Agents, Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, and 7x24 Mac Mini setups against the developer dilemma between leaving a Mac Mini running 24/7 causing thermal degradation/power bills vs using cloud managed agents that pause when idle but sacrifice Computer Use visual GUI interactions and lose installed environment dependencies upon container restart):** Myrm delivers a sub-second hibernation, dedicated persistent volume, and headless virtual display dual-mode Computer Use suite:
  1. **Idle 5-Minute Scale-to-Zero & <300ms Sub-Second Instant Thaw**: Upends traditional always-on VPS server billing and noisy Mac Mini 24/7 power drain. When an agent is idle for over 5 minutes, cgroup freezers pause CPU/GPU compute, reducing billing to zero; upon receiving inbound Webhooks, IM messages, or user inputs, the execution context is thawed in under 300ms;
  2. **Dedicated User Persistent Volume & Workspace State Keep**: Physical decoupling between compute instances and NVMe storage. Sandboxes can hibernate, restart, or migrate across physical cluster nodes without losing user-installed pip/npm packages, toolchain environments, local knowledge bases, or project assets;
  3. **Headless Virtual Display (Xvfb/Wayland) & WebRTC Dual-Mode Computer Use**: Solves the critical cloud VPS limitation of losing desktop visual control. Embeds lightweight Xvfb/Wayland virtual displays and low-latency WebRTC streaming directly in the cloud container, granting cloud agents 100% feature parity with local desktops for DOM/OCR multimodal screen interaction, clicking, and typing;
  4. **Host-Side Governed Egress Proxy & Blind Key Protection**: API keys are never injected into the sandbox container. LLM requests are signed and audited transparently via a host-side governed egress proxy, completely insulating master keys from untrusted community skills or shell scripts;
  5. **Local WebUI / Desktop / Cloud Tri-Deploy Isomorphism & Handoff**: Bridges the divide between local privacy and cloud elastic scale. Users can start complex tasks on a laptop, close the lid to hand off execution to an autonomous cloud sandbox, and later download the persistent volume back to a physical local Mac Mini.

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-02 · Dual-Flagship SOTA Blind Cross-Critique, 100% Consensus Synthesis & Light-Medium Zero-Wait Fast-Path Suite · Eliminating Single-Model Cognitive Hallucination Blindspots, Haphazard Switching & Daily Task Latency)

  1. **Dual-Flagship Heterogeneous Parallel Blind Cross-Critique**: When users toggle "✨ Dual-Flagship Consensus Mode" in WebUI or on mission-critical architecture decisions, queries are dispatched concurrently to top SOTA flagships (OpenAI and Claude); the engine automatically strips provider branding and dialect tokens, feeding Model A's output to Model B for adversarial audit and vice versa, dismantling single-model self-confirmation bias;
  2. **100% Consensus Extraction & Trade-Off Cards**: Automatically synthesizes 100% overlapping conclusions into high-confidence consensus deliverables, while structuring divergences into actionable Pros & Cons trade-off decision cards for instant user confirmation;

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-09-30 · Session-Scoped Adaptive Loop Scheduler, Target-Driven Termination & WebUI Floating Capsule Suite · Eliminating Idle Polling Token Burn, Runaway Loops, Black-Box Scheduling & Thundering Herd)

  3. **Homomorphic NLP Command Parsing & Immediate First-Run Verification**: Fixes legacy issues where leading `/loop`/`/repeat` prefixes bypassed interval extraction, introducing isomorphic regex-driven natural language duration parsing; triggers execution immediately on creation rather than waiting for the next cycle, allowing live visual inspection of agent execution before transitioning to seamless background persistence;

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-09-30 · Subagent Isolated SQLite Checkpointer Persistence, Read-Only Sandbox Fallback & SSOT Terminal Hygiene Suite · Eliminating Shared Checkpointer Lock Contention, Sandbox 500 Cold-Start Crashes, Unbounded DB Bloat & Python Shutdown Zombie Hangs)

  1. **Physically Isolated Subagent Checkpointer (`checkpointer.py`)**: Subagents use a dedicated, lightweight SQLite store (`subagent_checkpoints.sqlite`) completely isolated from the parent gateway session. Each subagent task maps to its own `thread_id == task_id`, preventing cross-agent thread collisions and eliminating multi-agent write lock contention;
  2. **Read-Only Sandbox Graceful In-Memory Fallback (`:memory:`)**: Intercepts `(OSError, aiosqlite.Error, sqlite3.OperationalError)` when running in restricted or read-only container volumes, automatically and gracefully degrading to an in-memory checkpointer with structured warnings to guarantee 0 cold-start crashes;
  3. **SSOT Terminal Hygiene & HITL Approval Retention (`drop_subagent_checkpoint_if_terminal`)**: Accurately retains checkpoint states when a subagent interrupts for human approval (`PENDING_APPROVAL`), enabling seamless cross-restart resumption via `Command(resume=...)`; automatically and asynchronously purges all non-approval terminal states (COMPLETED, FAILED, CANCELLED, TIMED_OUT) to prevent unbounded SQLite disk bloat;
  4. **Four-Layer Anti-Hang Shutdown Protection (Daemon Worker + Sync Stop + Atexit + SessionFinish)**: Solves the insidious Python `_thread_shutdown` hang caused by `aiosqlite` non-daemon worker threads. Sets `proxy._thread.daemon = True`, issues synchronous worker termination on reset, registers an `atexit` hook, and ties cleanup into `pytest_sessionfinish` for sub-second process exits;
  5. **100% Full-Stack Testing Verification**: 12/12 checkpointer unit tests passed in 3.73s with **94% statement coverage**, 22/22 approval integration tests passed in 3.94s, 68/68 orchestrator tests passed in 3.56s, and 131/131 subagent manager lifecycle tests passed in 142.94s (exit=0, 0 zombie processes).

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-09-29 · Live In-Meeting Structured Minutes, Idempotent Transcript Ingest & Collision-Proof Minute Archival Suite · Eliminating Post-Meeting Reconstruct, Duplicated Transcript Lines, Silently Dropped Meetings and Chatty-Model 500s)

- **LiveMeetingMinutesSuite (Benchmarking Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode across the in-meeting minutes loop, where competitors either summarize only after the meeting ends (DeepSeek-Harness whole-recording), stream subtitles without structure (Hermes), or have no meeting capability at all (Codex, Pi, OpenCode) — and OpenClaw, the only one with a real-time loop, still lacks idempotent ingest, in-meeting risk tracking, and archival into a searchable knowledge base):** Myrm delivers a complete in-meeting minutes loop that turns a live conversation into a structured, retrievable deliverable:
  1. **Rolling Structured Refresh In-Meeting**: Notes refresh during the conversation (bounded by a 200-character accumulation gate and a 120-second cadence) into five sections — summary, decisions, debate points, **risks**, and action items with owner and due hints — instead of waiting for a post-meeting batch;
  2. **At-Least-Once Safe Ingest**: Every transcript line carries a client-generated `line_id`; a replayed or retried request is ignored instead of duplicating lines, so the minutes and the line counter stay truthful (bounded 512-entry dedupe window keeps long meetings memory-flat);
  3. **Chatty-Model Tolerance**: Real LLMs return a bare string for a one-item list, `null` for "none", or a non-object payload. Each is normalized at the parse boundary — a single risk stays one risk instead of exploding into 23 single-character entries, `null` never raises, and a non-object payload degrades to a raw summary. A model that rambles can no longer turn into a failed meeting;
  4. **Non-Finite Timestamp Hardening**: An infinite client timestamp is rejected by the contract and clamped at the single write point, because `inf // 60` evaluates to NaN and used to abort finalization — losing the whole meeting;
  5. **Collision-Proof Archival**: The minutes filename is derived from the LLM-authored title, so a repeated title now falls through to `-2`, `-3`, and a random suffix instead of being silently skipped by the raw publication gate. Every meeting keeps its deliverable; nothing is overwritten;
  6. **Truthful Publish Contract**: The API returns vault-relative path strings for real writes only — never a serialized result object that leaked the host's absolute local path into the response body;
  7. **Automatic Knowledge Archival**: `finalize` forces a final distillation, publishes the minutes into the Wiki raw evidence layer with provenance frontmatter, and releases the session, so the deliverable is searchable and citable by the next person;
  8. **100% Full-Stack Testing Verification**: 196 server unit/API tests passed with changed-module coverage at 97% (`live_notes.py` 100%, `models.py` 100%, `service.py` 95%); 358 frontend tests passed including a new 10-case Live Board component suite and a new 17-case REST contract suite; a real-LLM, real-Chrome E2E drove the entire meeting flow inside the real WebUI session and asserted the published note is visible in the real UI, with zero residue.
- **SSOT Sources:** `myrm-agent/myrm-agent-server/app/services/meeting_notes/live_notes.py` · `.../meeting_notes/service.py` · `.../app/api/wiki/router.py` · `myrm-agent-frontend/src/hooks/voice/useLiveMeetingNotes.ts` · `.../components/features/voice/LiveNotesBoard.tsx` · `temp-docs/materials/OFFICE_DELIVERY_ADVANTAGE.md`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-09-28 · Preflight 71-Pattern Threat Scanner, 0-100 Quantitative Gate, AST Dangerous Call Penetration & Structured Audit Logging Suite · Eliminating Blind Trust in Third-Party Skills, Direct Ingestion of Malicious Jailbreak Code, Falsy Zero Escape & Untracked Administrative Overrides)

Deeply identifies hidden reverse shells, `eval`/`exec`/`__import__` dynamic obfuscations, env variable sniffing, directory traversal, and concealed prompt jailbreaks;

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-10-18 · Reversible Skill Upstream Updates & Shipped-Copy Integrity Gate · Eliminating «Apply Update» Being a One-Way Door and Machine-Translation Markers Reaching Readers)

- **ReversibleSkillUpdateAndCopyIntegritySuite (Benchmarking Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode across prebuilt-skill upstream updates and shipped localized copy, where upstream-vs-local reconciliation is either absent or has no user-facing undo, and where translation pipelines can leave placeholder markers in the strings users read):** Myrm closes both gaps with a per-update reversible path and a fail-closed copy gate:
  1. **Snapshot-Before-Overwrite with One-Click Undo**: Accepting an upstream update first writes the reader's own copy into a replace batch snapshot, then applies the bundled version. The confirmation says the update can be undone and the skill detail sheet then offers the undo, which restores the pre-update content (`myrm-agent-server/app/api/skills/prebuilt.py:134` · `SkillsSection.tsx:419-433`). The honest distinction matters: OpenClaw ships a versioned skill workshop with a revert command for *its own learned* skill changes (`claw/openclaw/src/skills/workshop/library.ts`); **Hermes has an undo primitive too** (skill consolidation archives instead of permanently deleting, so a misjudged consolidation can be undone — `hermes-agent/tools/skill_manager_tool.py:756`). What neither offers is a **per-update, per-skill undo tied to the bundled-upstream-versus-local-edit decision** — the case where an upgrade would otherwise discard work the reader wrote by hand;
  2. **Three-Way Hash Detection Instead of Blind Overwrite**: The sync pass compares stored content, the recorded origin, and the bundled source. Only the «reader edited **and** upstream moved» case is raised for review; a reader's untouched copy updates silently, and local edits are never silently replaced (`prebuilt_sync.py:276-312`);
  3. **Honest Confirmation in Every Language**: The confirmation's wording is asserted in German and English against the rendered page, so no locale can promise «cannot be undone» above a working undo control. A full Chrome run leaves the skill in the «upstream update pending» state, opens the detail sheet, raises the real dialog and reads its body (77.5s, session `SUCCEEDED`, cleanup sealed);
  4. **Fail-Closed Copy Gate Against Pipeline Artifacts**: `scanTranslationArtifacts` walks every leaf of all six locale trees and fails the ICU gate on leftover machine-translation markers (`__BR_…__` / `__PHn__`), so a broken translation pipeline cannot ship placeholder strings to readers. Measured green across **116,634** strings.
- **SSOT Sources:** `myrm-agent-server/app/api/skills/prebuilt.py` · `myrm-agent-server/app/core/skills/prebuilt_sync.py` · `myrm-agent-frontend/src/components/features/settings/sections/ai-tools/SkillsSection.tsx` · `myrm-agent-frontend/scripts/icu-syntax-scan.ts` · `temp-docs/materials/SKILL_MARKET_AND_ADOPTION_ADVANTAGE.md` · `temp-docs/materials/INTERNATIONALIZATION_LOCALIZATION_ADVANTAGE.md`

## Latest Benchmark Snapshot (2026-09-28 · Context Checkpoint Atomic Rollback & Safe Code Undo Suite · Eliminating Brain-Split Truncation, Virtual Scroll Index Drift, Irreversible State Loss & Disrupted Workflow)

- **ContextCheckpointAtomicRollbackAndUndoSuite (Benchmarking Hermes, OpenClaw, Codex, DeepSeek-Harness, Pi, and OpenCode across conversation history rollback, atomic state machine synchronization, and workspace code undo where competitors suffer from split-brain state corruption, lacking Pre-Rollback safety snapshots, virtual scroll index drift causing slice out-of-bounds, unmapped container file paths, and discarding user inputs):** Myrm delivers an enterprise-grade full-stack two-phase atomic safe rollback and code undo coordinator:
  1. **Two-Phase Safe Rollback Coordinator (Zero Brain-Split Guarantee)**: Phase 1 validates the target snapshot, captures a protective `PRE_ROLLBACK` auto-snapshot, and atomically restores workspace files. If any disk write or permission error occurs, the coordinator immediately aborts with 0 database modifications. Phase 2 transactionally truncates conversation messages and rewinds the LangGraph Checkpointer cursor only after code integrity is confirmed;
  2. **Dynamic SSOT Indexing & Virtual Scroll Drift Immunity**: Completely decouples modal triggers from local message array indices (`messages.slice(0, targetIndex)`). `RewindDialog` dynamically resolves the target message via authoritative `messageId` lookup against the central chat store, guaranteeing 100% slice accuracy across virtualized message lists, search filters, and lazy-loaded conversations;

Fully integrates `harden_connection_async` (WAL mode, 30s busy_timeout, NORMAL synchronous), guaranteeing zero deadlock or SQLite lock collision across concurrent subagents;
  3. **Strict Thread Isolation (`thread_id == task_id`)**: Enforces dedicated task namespace isolation where intermediate subagent exploratory steps and tool outputs never pollute or collide with the primary conversation session or primary checkpointer;

  6. **Unified Server Graceful Shutdown**: Seamlessly hooks `close_subagent_checkpointer` into the server's lifecycle shutdown protocol (`safe_close_checkpointer`), cleanly releasing underlying aiosqlite connection pools and async write locks to eliminate dangling threads or database corruption;

  4. **Startup Orphan Script Garbage Collector (`sweep_stale_materialized_scripts`)**: Automatically sweeps and reclaims abandoned temporary scripts older than 1 hour during session initialization and crash self-healing in `<0.2ms`. Features defensive `try-except OSError` wrapping to guarantee zero crashes even on restricted-permission sandbox mounts, eliminating volume disk leaks after OOM-killer SIGKILL terminations;
  5. **Zero-Breaking Metadata Propagation (`ArmoredCommandString`)**: Transparently tags command execution metadata via a native `str` subclass carrying `is_armored: bool`, propagating isolation status across `SessionExecutionResult` and frontend telemetry without breaking downstream string APIs;
  6. **100% Full-Stack Testing Verification**: 24 dedicated harness unit tests achieving **97.1% statement coverage**, 14 real-process session integration tests, and 10 extreme-edge tests (OOM truncation, signal broadcasting, stdin swallowing, and process group kills) 100% passed in safe isolated process groups with zero zombie remnants.
